    6.4  Common First-Time Setup Mistakes
         - Mistakes every first-time Backstage builder makes
         - How to spot them fast
         - How to avoid losing an afternoon to them
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MISTAKE                        WHY IT HAPPENS                    FIX                                    │
         │  ------------------------------  --------------------------------  ------------------------------        │
         │  Using SQLite, then wondering     Tutorials default to SQLite for   Switch to PostgreSQL (Ch 2.5,        │
         │   why prod breaks                  zero-setup demos                 26.2) before you go live             │
         │  Forgetting GITHUB_TOKEN scopes    Token created with no repo        Re-create with repo + read:org      │
         │                                     access, catalog fetch 404s        scopes (Ch 4.3)                    │
         │  catalog-info.yaml never appears   File pushed to a branch that      Point the Location at the           │
         │                                     is not the Location target        default branch, not a feature one  │
         │  Template scaffolds but the repo   Missing GITHUB_TOKEN with repo    Grant the publish:github action     │
         │   never gets created                creation permission               a token with repo scope            │
         │  TechDocs tab shows a 404          docs/ folder missing or           Add mkdocs.yml + docs/index.md,     │
         │                                     mkdocs.yml absent                 add the techdocs-ref annotation    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    6.5  What is Next — Growing Beyond the First Instance
         - Your first instance proves the concept; here is what to scale next
         - A concrete map from this working demo to the rest of the book
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ONE WORKING INSTANCE (this chapter) --> A REAL DEVELOPER PORTAL                              │
         │                                                                                               │
         │  More entities?          -->  Automate ingestion instead of hand-writing YAML   (Ch 7.6)      │
         │  More templates?         -->  Build a template library for every stack you own  (Ch 11-13)    │
         │  More teams onboarding?  -->  Add Groups, ownership, and ownership tags         (Ch 8.4, 23)  │
         │  Going to production?    -->  PostgreSQL, real SSO, TLS, backups               (Ch 26)        │
         │  Need custom UI?         -->  Build a plugin instead of using defaults         (Ch 16-18)     │
         │  Rolling out org-wide?   -->  CI/CD-enforced catalog-info.yaml on every repo    (Ch 25)       │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [UNDER THE HOOD]
       6.4's mistake table is really describing one underlying mechanism
       from 3.3: the entity lifecycle only starts once ingestion
       successfully fetches a Location target. If the Location points at
       a feature branch instead of the default branch (6.4), or the
       GitHub token lacks repo scope (6.4), ingestion never runs --
       Backstage does not error loudly here, it simply never surfaces the
       entity, which is why 6.3's checklist insists on actually LOOKING
       for jobpost in the Catalog rather than assuming a clean yarn dev
       startup means success.

  [KEY TAKEAWAYS]
       - Plan before you build: know your catalog seed, first template,
         and docs before running a command (6.1).
       - The 6-step build is: create app -> GitHub integration -> define
         entities -> register Location -> TechDocs -> K8s plugin (6.2).
       - "It didn't crash" is not verification -- use the 6.3 checklist
         and actually look for jobpost in the Catalog.
       - SQLite-for-prod, token scope, and wrong-branch Locations are the
         top first-time mistakes (6.4).
       - A missing docs/ folder or mkdocs.yml is the #1 cause of a
         TechDocs 404 (6.4).
       - One working instance is a proof of concept, not a rollout --
         later parts cover scaling entities, templates, and teams (6.5).

  [EXAM GOTCHAS]
       - Pointing a catalog Location at a feature branch instead of the
         default branch -- the entity silently never appears (6.4).
       - Creating a GitHub token with no repo or read:org scopes, then
         blaming Backstage for a 404 (6.4).
       - Treating a template that "scaffolds" as done -- if the token
         lacks repo-creation permission, the repo itself is never created
         (6.4).
       - Assuming yarn dev starting cleanly means the whole setup is
         correct (6.3).

  [SPEED TIPS]
       - Always run through the 6.3 checklist top to bottom after any
         first-time setup, before declaring it "done."
       - When TechDocs 404s, check docs/, then mkdocs.yml, then the
         techdocs-ref annotation, in that order (6.4).

  [COMMANDS / CONFIG TO REMEMBER]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ COMMANDS TO REMEMBER — Chapter 6                                              │
         │                                                                               │
         │ npx @backstage/create-app@latest         # 1. create the app (6.2)            │
         │ integrations: { github: [{ token: ${GITHUB_TOKEN} }] }   # 2. GitHub (6.2)    │
         │ apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1 / kind: Component      # 3. define (6.2)    │
         │ catalog: { locations: [{ type: url, target: <URL> }] }    # 4. register (6.2) │
         │ techdocs: { builder: local, publisher: { type: local } }  # 5. TechDocs (6.2) │
         │ yarn --cwd packages/app add @backstage/plugin-kubernetes   # 6. K8s (6.2)     │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [PRACTICE DRILL -- 20 minutes]

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
PART 3: SOFTWARE CATALOG — YOUR SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH               🟡
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

  [PART 3 INTRO]
  Part 2 got easypj running and gave it a config surface; Part 3 is where
  jobpost stops being "a repo you happen to know about" and becomes a first-
  class citizen of the Software Catalog — Backstage's single source of truth
  for every service, API, database, team and Kubernetes resource in the
  organization. This is also the single heaviest-weighted domain on the CBA
  exam (22% per chapter, four chapters straight), so what you build here pays
  off both on the job and on exam day.

  Chapter 7 opens the Catalog itself: how it ingests entities, where they can
  live, and how to debug the #1 real-world complaint ("my entity isn't
  showing up"). Chapter 8 names the 9 entity kinds and the relationships that
  connect them. Chapter 9 gets concrete: the exact YAML fields every
  catalog-info.yaml needs and how to validate one. Chapter 10 closes the loop
  by wiring a LIVE Kubernetes Deployment into the entity page, so jobpost's
  Catalog entry stops being static metadata and starts reflecting reality.

  🧠 ANALOGY. Think of the Software Catalog as a library's card catalog
  crossed with a live tracking system. Every book (entity) gets a card with
  its author (owner), genre (kind/type) and status (lifecycle) — but unlike a
  paper card catalog, this one restocks itself automatically (ingestion) and
  can tell you, right now, whether the book is even on the shelf (a
  Kubernetes pod actually running). Chapter 7 is learning how the shelves
  restock themselves; Chapters 8-9 are learning to read and write the cards
  correctly; Chapter 10 is what makes the "is it on the shelf right now"
  status real instead of guesswork.

  [HOW THE CHAPTERS FIT — Part 3 map]
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Ch7 CATALOG MECHANICS ──▶ Ch8 ENTITY KINDS ──▶ Ch9 YAML AUTHORING      │
  │   ingestion pipeline,        9 kinds, relation-      catalog-info.yaml  │
  │   locations & providers      ships, decision tree    structure & rules  │
  │                                                                         │
  │                      all three feed into                                │
  │                                                                         │
  │              Ch10: KUBERNETES INTEGRATION                               │
  │    wires a LIVE K8s Deployment onto the entity page                     │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Read it as a sentence: first understand HOW entities get into the Catalog
  and how to debug when they don't (Ch7), then learn WHAT kinds of entities
  exist and how they relate (Ch8), then learn to WRITE the YAML precisely
  (Ch9), and finally make it all LIVE by linking real Kubernetes state onto
  the entity page (Ch10).

  🧭 DECISION GUIDE — where do I start?
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ If you...                           │ Then...                                │
  ├─────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ have never seen the Catalog before  │ start at Ch7 -- don't skip 7.2 or 7.7  │
  │ already know what a Component is    │ skim Ch7, focus on Ch8 + Ch9           │
  │ need to register a real repo today  │ jump to 7.5/7.6, then Ch9 for the YAML │
  │ are already deploying to Kubernetes │ read Ch10 closely -- it assumes Ch7-9  │
  └─────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────┘

  [PART 3 SUMMARY + IMPLEMENTATION — read before the chapters]
  By the end of this part you will have registered jobpost in the Catalog
  with a complete catalog-info.yaml (Component + API + Resource + Group
  entities, correctly related), enabled automated GitHub org discovery for
  easypj so new repos register themselves, and linked jobpost's real
  Kubernetes Deployment to its entity page so its pod/service/ingress status
  shows up live, right next to its ownership and documentation.

Chapter 7: The Catalog — Central Nervous System of Backstage 🎯 CBA 22% (Catalog)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    [META] 🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~55min · CBA Domain: 22% (Catalog)

    [WHY THIS MATTERS]
         Once easypj/jobpost is deployed, it's just one more repo among
         dozens unless something turns it into a discoverable, ownership-
         tagged entry other engineers can find. The Catalog is that thing —
         it is the reason a new hire can search "jobpost" and immediately see
         who owns it, what it depends on, and whether it's healthy, instead
         of asking around in chat.

    [OBJECTIVES — after this chapter you can:]
         ✅ explain what the Software Catalog is and why it's the single
            source of truth for every service, API and team;
         ✅ describe the ingestion pipeline: Location/Provider -> Processor ->
            Catalog DB -> API;
         ✅ register jobpost manually via the UI wizard, app-config.yaml, or
            the Catalog API;
         ✅ configure automated GitHub org discovery so new easypj repos
            register themselves;
         ✅ navigate the Catalog UI — grid view, entity page tabs, search and
            the dependency graph;
         ✅ diagnose a missing-entity problem using the 5-step ingestion
            debug flowchart.

    [CBA RELEVANCE]
         Catalog is the single largest domain on the CBA exam at 22% per
         chapter, spanning Chapters 7-10 — this chapter is its foundation.

    [SECTION MAP — you are here:]
         7.1 What is the Catalog -> 7.2 How It Works (Under the Hood) ->
         7.3 Locations -> 7.4 Viewing & Exploring -> 7.5 Manual Registration
         -> 7.6 Automated Ingestion -> 7.7 Troubleshooting Ingestion -> 7.8
         Practice Questions.

    7.1  What is the Software Catalog?
         - The single source of truth for ALL your software
         - Tracks: Services, APIs, Websites, Libraries, Databases, Teams
         - Auto-discovers from GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket, K8s
         - Shows: Ownership, documentation, dependencies, health status
         - Updates automatically — no manual data entry
         [BIG PICTURE DIAGRAM: Catalog as the central hub]

         [THE CATALOG — Your Software Nervous System]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │   GitHub ───┐                                               │
         │   GitLab ───┤                                               │
         │   K8s ──────┤──▶  📦 SOFTWARE CATALOG  ──▶  🔍 SEARCH       │
         │   Jenkins ──┤         (PostgreSQL)            🖥️  DASHBOARD  │
         │   Sentry ───┤                                📖 TECHDOCS    │
         │   PagerDuty ┤                                🔌 PLUGINS     │
         │                                                              │
         │   Everything auto-discovered, always up-to-date!            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.2  How the Catalog Works — Under the Hood
         - Catalog ingestion pipeline
         - Entity providers: Where entities come from
         - Processors: How entities are transformed
         - Database: Where entities are stored
         - API: How entities are queried
         [FLOW DIAGRAM: Ingestion pipeline stages]

         [CATALOG INGESTION PIPELINE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  📥 CATALOG INGESTION FLOW                                   │
         │                                                              │
         │  LOCATION             PROVIDER          PROCESSOR            │
         │  ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────┐         │
         │  │ GitHub   │──────▶│ GithubEn │──────▶│ Placehol │         │
         │  │ Repo URL │       │ tityProv │       │ derProc  │         │
         │  └──────────┘       └──────────┘       └──────────┘         │
         │                                              │               │
         │  ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────┐            ▼               │
         │  │ YAML     │──────▶│ UrlReade │       ┌──────────┐         │
         │  │ File     │       │ r        │       │ EntityPr │         │
         │  └──────────┘       └──────────┘       │ ocessor  │         │
         │                                         └──────────┘         │
         │                                              │               │
         │  ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────┐            ▼               │
         │  │ K8s      │──────▶│ Kubernet │       ┌──────────┐         │
         │  │ Cluster  │       │ esEntity │       │ 💾  DB   │         │
         │  └──────────┘       │ Provider │       │ STORE    │         │
         │                     └──────────┘       └──────────┘         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.3  Catalog Locations — Where Entities Live
         - URL locations: Point to GitHub/GitLab repos with catalog-info.yaml
         - File locations: Local files for development
         - Integration-based: Auto-discovered from K8s, AWS, etc.
         - Configuring locations in app-config.yaml
         [TABLE: Location types with examples]

         [REAL-LIFE: Registering easypj in the Catalog — 3 Methods]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  METHOD 1: GitHub URL (most common in production)            │
         │  catalog.locations: [{type: url, target:                    │
         │    https://github.com/easypj/jobpost/blob/main/catalog-info  │
         │    .yaml}]                                                    │
         │  METHOD 2: Local file (for dev): type: file, target: ./      │
         │  METHOD 3: GitHub Org auto-discovery (bulk):                 │
         │    catalog.providers.github.organization: easypj             │
         │    Auto-finds ALL repos with catalog-info.yaml!              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [REAL-LIFE: Verifying Catalog Setup]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. Go to http://localhost:3000/catalog                      │
         │  2. See "jobpost" in the component list                      │
         │  3. Click it → Overview, Docs, API, Dependencies tabs        │
         │  4. Search: type "jobpost" in search bar                     │
         │  Troubleshooting: If entity not appearing:                   │
         │  $ curl http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/entities           │
         │  → Check YAML syntax, remove trailing commas                 │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    7.4  Viewing and Exploring the Catalog
         - Catalog page: Grid view, table view
         - Entity page: Overview, Docs, API, Dependencies tabs
         - Filtering: By kind, owner, lifecycle, tags
         - Search: Full-text search across all entities
         - Dependency graph: Visual representation

    7.5  Working with Manually Registered Entity Locations
         - A manual Location is a single, explicit pointer YOU add — nothing
           discovers it automatically. Two equivalent ways to add one:
         - (1) UI: click Create > Register Existing Component, paste the URL
         - (2) YAML: add an entry under catalog.locations in app-config.yaml
         - (3) API: POST to /api/catalog/locations with a target URL
         - Manual registration is best for: one-off entities, early testing,
           or repos an automated discovery scan wouldn't reach

         [REGISTERING jobpost MANUALLY — 3 Equivalent Methods]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  METHOD 1: The UI Wizard (fastest for one entity)                             │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────                            │
         │  1. Click CREATE in the sidebar                                               │
         │  2. Click Register Existing Component (bottom of the template list)           │
         │  3. Paste: https://github.com/easypj/jobpost/blob/main/catalog-info.yaml      │
         │  4. Backstage fetches + PREVIEWS the entity before you confirm                │
         │  5. Click Import — entity now appears in the Catalog                          │
         │                                                                               │
         │  METHOD 2: app-config.yaml (best for a fixed, known set of repos)             │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────            │
         │  catalog:                                                                     │
         │    locations:                                                                 │
         │      - type: url                                                              │
         │        target: https://github.com/easypj/jobpost/blob/main/catalog-info.yaml  │
         │                                                                               │
         │  METHOD 3: Catalog API (best for scripting/automation)                        │
         │  ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────                     │
         │  curl -X POST http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/locations \                   │
         │    -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \                                      │
         │    -d '{"type":"url","target":"https://github.com/easypj/jobpost/             │
         │         blob/main/catalog-info.yaml"}'                                        │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 🔑 A Location just POINTS to a file — it does NOT create
         the entity itself. The entity only appears after the Catalog fetches
         and successfully PROCESSES the YAML at that location.

    7.6  Working with Automated Ingestion — Discovery & Providers
         - Manual Locations don't scale past a handful of repos. AUTOMATED
           ingestion uses an EntityProvider to discover MANY entities on a
           recurring schedule — no one has to add each repo by hand
         - GithubDiscoveryProcessor / GitHub Organization Provider scans an
           entire GitHub org for catalog-info.yaml files matching a glob
         - Similar discovery providers exist for GitLab, Bitbucket, AWS, and
           Kubernetes clusters (auto-registering Deployments as entities)
         - Providers run on a SCHEDULE (e.g. every 30 minutes) and diff what
           they find against what's already in the Catalog — new repos appear
           automatically, deleted repos are marked as orphaned

         [MANUAL LOCATION vs AUTOMATED PROVIDER]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MANUAL LOCATION                        AUTOMATED PROVIDER                 │
         │  ─────────────────────────────────      ─────────────────────────────────  │
         │  You add ONE explicit URL                Scans an ENTIRE org/account       │
         │  Static — never finds new repos          Finds NEW repos automatically     │
         │  Good for: 1-5 known repos                Good for: 50+ repos, whole orgs  │
         │  Config: catalog.locations                Config: catalog.providers.*      │
         │  Runs once when Backstage starts          Runs on a recurring SCHEDULE     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [CONFIGURING GitHub ORG AUTO-DISCOVERY for easypj]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                                                            │
         │  catalog:                                                                     │
         │    providers:                                                                 │
         │      github:                                                                  │
         │        providerId:                                                            │
         │          organization: 'easypj'                                               │
         │          catalogPath: '/catalog-info.yaml'   # file to look for in each repo  │
         │          filters:                                                             │
         │            repository: '.*'                  # regex — which repos to scan    │
         │          schedule:                                                            │
         │            frequency: { minutes: 30 }         # how often to re-scan          │
         │            timeout: { minutes: 3 }                                            │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 💡 CBA Tip — Provider vs Processor: a PROVIDER finds
         WHICH entities exist (discovery). A PROCESSOR transforms/enriches
         an entity ONCE it's already been found (e.g. resolving placeholders,
         validating schema). Every entity passes through processors —
         only some entities arrive via a provider instead of a manual Location.

    7.7  Troubleshooting Entity Ingestion — A Systematic Method
         - "My entity isn't showing up" is the #1 real-world Catalog problem
         - Debug in this EXACT order — each step rules out one entire cause

         [THE 5-STEP INGESTION DEBUG FLOWCHART]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STEP 1 — Is the Location even configured?                              │
         │  Check catalog.locations / catalog.providers in app-config.yaml.        │
         │  No location = Backstage never even LOOKS for your YAML.                │
         │       │ if configured, continue ▼                                       │
         │  STEP 2 — Can Backstage REACH the file?                                 │
         │  curl the raw file yourself. Private repo? Check the integration token  │
         │  in app-config.yaml has 'repo' scope and hasn't expired.                │
         │       │ if reachable, continue ▼                                        │
         │  STEP 3 — Is the YAML actually VALID?                                   │
         │  Run it through a YAML linter. Common killers: tabs instead of spaces,  │
         │  missing apiVersion/kind, a trailing comma copied from JSON examples.   │
         │       │ if valid, continue ▼                                            │
         │  STEP 4 — Has ingestion actually RUN yet?                               │
         │  Force it: curl -X POST localhost:7007/api/catalog/refresh              │
         │    -d '{"entityRef":"component:default/jobpost"}'                       │
         │  Or just wait for the next scheduled processing interval.               │
         │       │ if refreshed, continue ▼                                        │
         │  STEP 5 — Check the backend LOGS for the real error                     │
         │  Ingestion failures are logged, not silently swallowed. Look for        │
         │  'PlaceholderResolutionError', 'InputError', or 'NotFoundError'.        │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [TROUBLESHOOTING COMMANDS — Your Debug Toolkit]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WHAT YOU WANT TO KNOW              COMMAND                                      │
         │  ──────────────────────────────     ──────────────────────────────────────       │
         │  Does this entity exist at all?      curl localhost:7007/api/catalog/            │
         │                                      entities/by-name/component/default/jobpost  │
         │  What locations are registered?      curl localhost:7007/api/catalog/locations   │
         │  Force a re-ingestion right now      curl -X POST localhost:7007/api/catalog/    │
         │                                      refresh -d entityRef=...                    │
         │  See ALL entities (sanity check)     curl localhost:7007/api/catalog/entities    │
         │  Watch ingestion happen live         Tail the backend logs while refreshing      │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [COMMON INGESTION FAILURES — Root Cause Reference]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYMPTOM                          ROOT CAUSE                    FIX             │
         │  ────────────────────────────     ─────────────────────────    ─────────────    │
         │  Entity never appears             Location not configured        Add to         │
         │                                                                   catalog.*     │
         │  "404" fetching the YAML         Wrong branch/path in the URL   Fix the URL     │
         │  "401/403" fetching the YAML     Missing/expired GitHub token   Rotate token    │
         │  Entity appears then vanishes     Provider re-scan can't find    Repo renamed/  │
         │                                    it anymore                    deleted        │
         │  Entity shows but fields blank     YAML valid but incomplete      Add missing   │
         │                                                                   spec fields   │
         │  Two entities collide              Duplicate metadata.name        Rename one    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 🎯 CBA Exam Pattern — a scenario question almost always
         boils down to ONE of these 5 steps. "Entity registered a week ago
         but never appeared" = Step 1 (never configured) or Step 3 (invalid
         YAML), never Step 5 — the exam rewards ruling out the SIMPLE causes
         first, not jumping straight to logs.

    [UNDER THE HOOD 🔧]
         Every entity's journey is the same regardless of how it entered: a
         Location or an EntityProvider tells the Catalog WHERE to look (7.3,
         7.6); a Processor then transforms and enriches what it finds —
         resolving placeholders, validating schema — before anything is
         persisted (7.2); only after that does the entity land in the
         Catalog's PostgreSQL database and become queryable through the API
         and visible in the UI. A Provider only ever answers "which entities
         exist"; a Processor answers "is this one valid, and what does it
         mean" — every entity passes through a Processor, but only some
         arrive via a Provider instead of a hand-added Location.

    [KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑]
         🔑 The Catalog is the single source of truth — services, APIs,
            websites, libraries, databases and teams all live in it.
         🔑 Ingestion is always Location/Provider -> Processor -> DB -> API.
         🔑 Manual registration has 3 equivalent forms: the UI wizard,
            app-config.yaml locations, and a Catalog API POST.
         🔑 Automated ingestion (an EntityProvider) scans an entire org on a
            recurring schedule — it scales where manual Locations don't.
         🔑 A Location only POINTS to a file; it does not create the entity —
            the entity appears only after successful processing.
         🔑 "Entity isn't showing up" is always debugged in the same 5-step
            order: configured? -> reachable? -> valid? -> ingested? -> logs.

    [EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️]
         ⚠️ Assuming an entity appears with NO Location or Provider
            configured at all — nothing is automatic without one.
         ⚠️ Jumping straight to backend logs (Step 5) before ruling out the
            simpler Steps 1-4 — the exam rewards ruling out simple causes
            first.
         ⚠️ Confusing a Provider (discovers WHICH entities exist) with a
            Processor (transforms an entity already found).
         ⚠️ Forgetting that a private repo needs a token with 'repo' scope —
            a 401/403 almost always means an expired or missing token.

    [SPEED TIPS ⚡]
         ⚡ curl /api/catalog/entities first as a sanity check that ANYTHING
            is registered before debugging a single entity.
         ⚡ Force a re-ingestion with the /refresh endpoint instead of
            waiting for the next scheduled scan.
         ⚡ Past a handful of repos, always prefer GitHub org auto-discovery
            over hand-adding Locations one at a time.

    [COMMANDS TO REMEMBER 🔑]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  curl http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/entities                                    │
         │  curl http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/entities/by-name/component/default/jobpost  │
         │  curl http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/locations                                   │
         │  curl -X POST http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/locations \                         │
         │    -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \                                            │
         │    -d '{"type":"url","target":"<catalog-info.yaml URL>"}'                           │
         │  curl -X POST localhost:7007/api/catalog/refresh \                                  │
         │    -d '{"entityRef":"component:default/jobpost"}'                                   │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [PRACTICE DRILL 🧪]
         🧪 In 20 minutes:
         1. Register jobpost manually via the UI wizard (5 min).
         2. Configure GitHub org auto-discovery for easypj in
            app-config.yaml (5 min).
         3. Deliberately break its catalog-info.yaml and walk the 5-step
            debug flowchart to find the break (7 min).
         4. curl the Catalog API to confirm jobpost is registered again
            (3 min).

    7.8  CBA Exam: Chapter 7 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Catalog concepts, ingestion, locations, providers,
           manual registration, and troubleshooting ingestion failures

    [VISUAL SUMMARY — Chapter 7: "From scattered repos to one searchable Catalog"]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  GitHub / GitLab / K8s / Jenkins / Sentry / PagerDuty            │
         │                           │                                      │
         │           ▼ Location (manual, 7.5) or Provider (auto, 7.6)       │
         │                           │                                      │
         │                           ▼                                      │
         │                 Processor (transforms/enriches, 7.2)             │
         │                           │                                      │
         │                           ▼                                      │
         │                💾 Catalog DB  ──▶  🔍 Search / 🖥️ UI / API      │
         │                                                                  │
         │    Nothing appearing? Walk the 7.7 debug flow in order:          │
         │    location? ─▶ reachable? ─▶ valid YAML? ─▶ ingested? ─▶ logs?  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    .vs-prose — In words: entities reach the Catalog from many sources — a
    manually added Location, an automated Provider scanning GitHub, or the
    Kubernetes plugin (Ch10). Whichever path it takes, every entity passes
    through a Processor that validates and enriches it before it's stored and
    made searchable. When an entity doesn't show up, the fix is never a
    guess — walk the 5-step flow from 7.7 in order, and you'll find whether
    it's a missing Location, an unreachable repo, invalid YAML, unrun
    ingestion, or a real error in the logs.


Chapter 8: Catalog Entities — Kinds, Types & Lifecycle 🎯 CBA 22% (Catalog)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    [META] 🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~40min · CBA Domain: 22% (Catalog)

    [WHY THIS MATTERS]
         Registering jobpost as a bare Component isn't enough to make the
         Catalog useful — without modeling its API, its database, its owning
         team and the system it belongs to, the Catalog degrades into a flat,
         meaningless list of names. This chapter is what makes jobpost's
         entry actually informative.

    [OBJECTIVES — after this chapter you can:]
         ✅ list the 9 entity kinds and what real-world thing each one
            represents;
         ✅ write a complete Component entity for jobpost with the right
            spec fields;
         ✅ distinguish a System from a Domain and know when to create each;
         ✅ model ownership and team structure with User and Group entities,
            including group hierarchy;
         ✅ define a Resource entity and link it to a Component with
            dependsOn;
         ✅ map every YAML relationship field to its auto-generated graph
            relation name;
         ✅ pick the correct entity kind for any artifact using the decision
            tree.

    [CBA RELEVANCE]
         Catalog domain, 22% — this chapter covers the entity model tested
         throughout Chapters 7-10 and referenced again whenever an exam
         scenario asks "what kind should this be?"

    [SECTION MAP — you are here:]
         8.1 Component -> 8.2 API -> 8.3 System & Domain -> 8.4 User & Group
         -> 8.5 Resource -> 8.6 Entity Relationships -> 8.7 Practice
         Questions.

    8.1  Component Entity Deep Dive
         - What counts as a Component: services, websites, libraries, CLI tools
         - Component types: service, website, library, CLI
         - Component lifecycle: experimental → production → deprecated
         - Owner: The team/user responsible
         - System: The larger system this component belongs to
         [CODE BLOCK: Complete Component YAML for easypj/jobpost]

         [COMPONENT ENTITY — jobpost Example]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # catalog-info.yaml for easypj/jobpost                     │
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                           │
         │  kind: Component                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: jobpost                                             │
         │    description: Job posting and management application       │
         │    annotations:                                              │
         │      github.com/project-slug: easypj/jobpost                 │
         │      backstage.io/techdocs-ref: dir:.                        │
         │    tags: [python, django, postgresql]                        │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    type: service                                             │
         │    lifecycle: production                                     │
         │    owner: team-platform                                      │
         │    system: easypj-recruitment-system                         │
         │    providesApis:                                             │
         │      - jobpost-api                                           │
         │    dependsOn:                                                │
         │      - resource:postgres-db                                  │
         │      - resource:redis-cache                                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.2  API Entity Deep Dive
         - Defining APIs in Backstage
         - API types: openapi, asyncapi, graphql, grpc
         - Linking APIs to Components: providesApis / consumesApis
         - Auto-generating API specs from code
         [API ENTITY — jobpost-api Example]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                                     │
         │  kind: API                                                             │
         │  metadata:                                                             │
         │    name: jobpost-api                                                   │
         │    description: REST API for job postings — filter by skills/location  │
         │  spec:                                                                 │
         │    type: openapi                                                       │
         │    lifecycle: production                                               │
         │    owner: team-platform                                                │
         │    system: easypj-system                                               │
         │    definition:                                                         │
         │      $text: https://github.com/easypj/jobpost/blob/main/openapi.yaml   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.3  System & Domain Entities
         - System: Groups related Components and Resources
         - Domain: Groups related Systems (business domain)
         - When to create Systems vs Domains
         - Example: easypj-recruitment-system, hr-domain
         [SYSTEM & DOMAIN HIERARCHY]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  hr-domain (Domain)  — the business area                           │
         │    └── easypj-system (System) — groups everything below            │
         │          ├── jobpost (Component: service)                          │
         │          ├── jobpost-api (API: openapi)                            │
         │          ├── postgres-db (Resource: database)                      │
         │          └── redis-cache (Resource: cache)                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  RULE OF THUMB: create a System when 2+ Components/Resources work  │
         │  together as one product. Create a Domain only when you have       │
         │  MULTIPLE Systems that belong to the same business area.           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.4  User & Group Entities
         - User: Represents a person (auto-discovered from auth provider)
         - Group: Represents a team, squad, or org unit
         - Group hierarchy: Parent/child relationships
         - Mapping to your org chart: teams, squads, tribes, guilds
         [GROUP ENTITY — team-platform Example]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1         │
         │  kind: Group                               │
         │  metadata:                                 │
         │    name: team-platform                     │
         │    description: Platform engineering team  │
         │  spec:                                     │
         │    type: team                              │
         │    profile:                                │
         │      displayName: Platform Team            │
         │      email: platform@easypj.com            │
         │    children: []                            │
         │    members: [jane.doe, mike.smith]         │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.5  Resource Entity
         - What counts as a Resource: databases, queues, caches, buckets
         - Resource types: database, s3-bucket, pubsub-topic
         - Linking Resources to Components: dependsOn
         - Example: postgres-db, redis-cache for easypj
         [RESOURCE ENTITY — postgres-db Example]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                       │
         │  kind: Resource                                          │
         │  metadata:                                               │
         │    name: postgres-db                                     │
         │    description: Primary PostgreSQL database for jobpost  │
         │  spec:                                                   │
         │    type: database                                        │
         │    owner: team-platform                                  │
         │    system: easypj-system                                 │
         │    dependencyOf: [component:jobpost]                     │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    8.6  Entity Relationships — The Big Picture
         - Ownership: owner field (User or Group)
         - Part-of: system, subcomponentOf
         - API relationships: providesApis, consumesApis
         - Dependency: dependsOn, dependencyOf
         - Parent/Child: parent, children (for Groups)
         [RELATIONSHIP MAP DIAGRAM]

         [easypj ENTITY RELATIONSHIP MAP]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │              ┌─────────────────────┐                         │
         │              │  🏢 hr-domain       │ ← Domain                │
         │              └──────────┬──────────┘                         │
         │                         │                                    │
         │              ┌──────────▼──────────┐                         │
         │              │  🖥️ easypj-system   │ ← System                │
         │              └──────────┬──────────┘                         │
         │                         │                                    │
         │        ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐                   │
         │        │                │                 │                   │
         │  ┌─────▼─────┐   ┌──────▼──────┐   ┌─────▼─────┐            │
         │  │ 📦 jobpost│   │ 💾 postgres │   │ 🔴 redis  │            │
         │  │ Component │──▶│  Resource   │   │ Resource  │            │
         │  │ (service) │   │ (database)  │   │ (cache)   │            │
         │  └─────┬─────┘   └─────────────┘   └───────────┘            │
         │        │                                                     │
         │        │ providesApis                                        │
         │        ▼                                                     │
         │  ┌─────────────┐                                             │
         │  │ 📡 jobpost  │ ← API                                      │
         │  │    -api     │                                             │
         │  │ (openapi)   │                                             │
         │  └─────────────┘                                             │
         │                                                              │
         │  Owner: 👥 team-platform (Group)                             │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ENTITY RELATIONSHIP TYPES — Complete Reference Map]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔗 ENTITY RELATIONSHIP TYPES — How Entities Connect         │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
         │  │ RELATIONSHIP         DIRECTION       EXAMPLE          │  │
         │  │──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ owner                Component→Group  jobpost owned   │  │
         │  │                      API→Group        by team-platform│  │
         │  │                      System→Group                      │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ system               Component→System jobpost part of│  │
         │  │                                         easypj-system │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ domain               System→Domain    easypj-system   │  │
         │  │                                         in hr-domain  │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ providesApis         Component→API    jobpost provides│  │
         │  │                                         jobpost-api   │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ consumesApis         Component→API    frontend consumes│  │
         │  │                                         jobpost-api   │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ dependsOn            Component→Resource jobpost depends│  │
         │  │                      Component→Component on postgres-db│  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ dependencyOf         Resource→Component  postgres-db is│  │
         │  │                      Component→Component dependency of │  │
         │  │                                         jobpost        │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ parent/children      Group→Group       team-platform   │  │
         │  │                                         parent of      │  │
         │  │                                         team-fe, team- │  │
         │  │                                         data           │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ memberOf             User→Group        jane.doe member│  │
         │  │                                         of team-platf. │  │
         │  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────│  │
         │  │ subcomponentOf       Component→Component jobpost-admin │  │
         │  │                                         subcomponent  │  │
         │  │                                         of jobpost    │  │
         │  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 KEY RULE: owner is MANDATORY for Component, API,         │
         │     System, Resource, Domain. All other relationships are    │
         │     optional but recommended for full visibility.            │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 🔑 CBA Precision — Spec Field Name ≠ Relation Type Name.
         The table above shows SPEC fields (what you write in YAML). Backstage
         auto-generates a DIFFERENT pair of relation names in the catalog
         graph itself: spec.owner produces relation ownedBy / ownerOf;
         spec.system, spec.domain and spec.subcomponentOf all produce
         partOf / hasPart; spec.providesApis/consumesApis produce
         apiProvidedBy/providesApi and apiConsumedBy/consumesApi (singular
         relation names, even though the YAML fields are plural arrays);
         spec.dependsOn produces dependsOn/dependencyOf; spec.memberOf
         produces memberOf/hasMember; and Group parent/children produce
         parentOf/childOf. The exam can ask for either the YAML field name
         or the underlying graph relation name — know both.

         [ENTITY KIND DECISION TREE — Which Kind Should You Use?]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🌳 CHOOSING THE RIGHT ENTITY KIND                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Is it a piece of software?"                                │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ├── YES → 📦 Component                                  │
         │      │         (service, website, library, CLI)              │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ├── "Is it an API definition?"                          │
         │      │   YES → 📡 API (openapi, asyncapi, graphql, grpc)     │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ├── "Is it infrastructure?"                             │
         │      │   YES → 💾 Resource (database, cache, queue, bucket)  │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      └── "Is it an organizational unit?"                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Is it a person?"                                           │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ├── YES → 👤 User                                       │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      └── NO → "Is it a team/squad?"                          │
         │              YES → 👥 Group                                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Is it a grouping of Components + Resources?"               │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ├── YES → 🖥️ System                                     │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      └── "Is it a grouping of Systems?"                      │
         │              YES → 🏢 Domain                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  "Is it a blueprint for creating projects?"                  │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      └── YES → 🚀 Template                                   │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [UNDER THE HOOD 🔧]
         Every relationship you write in spec has a mirror-image relation
         name that Backstage generates automatically in the catalog graph:
         spec.owner produces ownedBy/ownerOf; spec.system, spec.domain and
         spec.subcomponentOf all produce partOf/hasPart; providesApis /
         consumesApis produce apiProvidedBy/providesApi and
         apiConsumedBy/consumesApi; dependsOn produces dependsOn/
         dependencyOf; memberOf produces memberOf/hasMember; and Group
         parent/children produce parentOf/childOf. The YAML field name and
         the graph relation name are not always the same word — the exam can
         ask for either.

    [KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑]
         🔑 9 entity kinds total: Component, API, System, Domain, Resource,
            User, Group, Location, Template.
         🔑 Component's type field distinguishes service/website/library/CLI;
            lifecycle tracks experimental -> production -> deprecated.
         🔑 owner is MANDATORY on Component, API, System, Resource and
            Domain.
         🔑 System groups Components + Resources that ship together; Domain
            groups multiple Systems in the same business area.
         🔑 Group models teams with a parent/children hierarchy; User is
            usually auto-discovered from the auth provider, not hand-written.
         🔑 Every spec relationship field has a paired auto-generated graph
            relation name (8.6's Info Box).
         🔑 Use the entity kind decision tree (8.6) whenever unsure which of
            the 9 kinds applies.

    [EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️]
         ⚠️ Forgetting owner is mandatory on Component/API/System/Resource/
            Domain — a missing owner is a common validation failure.
         ⚠️ Confusing System (groups Components/Resources) with Domain
            (groups Systems).
         ⚠️ Mixing up a relationship's direction — dependsOn vs
            dependencyOf, providesApis vs consumesApis.
         ⚠️ Mixing up the YAML field name (e.g. providesApis) with its
            underlying relation name (apiProvidedBy/providesApi).

    [SPEED TIPS ⚡]
         ⚡ Create a System as soon as 2+ Components/Resources ship together
            as one product — don't wait until it's messy.
         ⚡ Reach for the 8.6 decision tree first whenever you're unsure
            between Component/Resource/System for something new.

    [COMMANDS/CONFIG TO REMEMBER 🔑]
         ┌───────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │ KIND      │ OWNER REQUIRED? / KEY SPEC FIELDS                   │
         ├───────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
         │ Component │ YES -- type, lifecycle, owner, system, providesApis │
         │ API       │ YES -- type, lifecycle, owner, system, definition   │
         │ Resource  │ YES -- type, owner, system, dependsOn               │
         │ System    │ YES -- owner, domain                                │
         │ Domain    │ YES -- owner                                        │
         │ User      │ no -- profile (email, displayName), memberOf        │
         │ Group     │ no -- type, profile, parent, children, members      │
         └───────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [PRACTICE DRILL 🧪]
         🧪 In 20 minutes:
         1. Write a Component entity for a new easypj service (5 min).
         2. Write matching API and Resource entities and link them with
            providesApis / dependsOn (8 min).
         3. Decide System vs Domain for your own org using the rule of thumb
            (8.3) (3 min).
         4. Trace one relationship (e.g. dependsOn) to its auto-generated
            graph relation name (4 min).

    8.7  CBA Exam: Chapter 8 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Entity kinds, relationships, YAML structure, types

    [VISUAL SUMMARY — Chapter 8: "easypj's entities, related"]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🏢 hr-domain (Domain)                                                  │
         │     └── 🖥️ easypj-system (System)                                      │
         │            ├── 📦 jobpost (Component: service, owner: team-platform)    │
         │            │      ├─ providesApis ─▶ 📡 jobpost-api (API: openapi)      │
         │            │      └─ dependsOn ────▶ 💾 postgres-db (Resource)          │
         │            └── 👥 team-platform (Group, owner of everything above)      │
         │                                                                         │
         │    Pick the kind with the decision tree (8.6): software? -> Component;  │
         │    API def? -> API; infra? -> Resource; grouping? -> System/Domain;     │
         │    person/team? -> User/Group.                                          │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    .vs-prose — In words: jobpost is a Component owned by team-platform,
    grouped under the easypj-system System which itself sits inside the
    hr-domain Domain. It provides an API (jobpost-api) and depends on a
    Resource (postgres-db). Every one of those links — owner, system,
    providesApis, dependsOn — is a spec field you write in YAML (Ch9) and a
    graph relation Backstage generates automatically underneath it. When in
    doubt about which of the 9 kinds a new artifact should be, the decision
    tree in 8.6 always gets you to the right answer in a few questions.


Chapter 9: Catalog YAML & Descriptors — Writing catalog-info.yaml 🎯 CBA 22% (Catalog)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    [META] 🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~35min · CBA Domain: 22% (Catalog)

    [WHY THIS MATTERS]
         catalog-info.yaml is the one file every easypj repo needs to join
         the Catalog. Get its structure wrong — a missing apiVersion, a
         typo'd kind — and jobpost silently fails to ingest, which is exactly
         the kind of problem Chapter 7.7 taught you to debug. This chapter is
         what stops that problem happening in the first place.

    [OBJECTIVES — after this chapter you can:]
         ✅ name the 4 required top-level fields of any catalog-info.yaml;
         ✅ fill out the metadata block correctly, including the difference
            between labels, tags and annotations;
         ✅ write the correct spec fields for each of the 7 documented
            entity kinds;
         ✅ use standard annotations to wire an entity to GitHub, TechDocs
            and other tools;
         ✅ split or combine entities in one file with the --- separator;
         ✅ validate a catalog-info.yaml before and after it's committed.

    [CBA RELEVANCE]
         Catalog domain, 22% — this chapter is the most literal, YAML-level
         test of the entity model taught in Chapter 8.

    [SECTION MAP — you are here:]
         9.1 Structure -> 9.2 Metadata -> 9.3 Spec -> 9.4 Annotations -> 9.5
         Multi-Entity Files -> 9.6 Validating -> 9.7 Practice Questions.

    9.1  catalog-info.yaml Structure
         - The 4 required top-level fields
         - apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1
         - kind: Component | API | System | Domain | Resource | User | Group | Location | Template  (9 kinds total)
         - metadata: name, description, annotations, labels, tags, links
         - spec: type-specific properties
         [ANATOMY OF A catalog-info.yaml FILE]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1   ← ALWAYS this exact value     │
         │  kind: Component                    ← ONE of the 9 valid kinds     │
         │  metadata:                          ← Identity + discovery         │
         │    name: jobpost                     (REQUIRED — unique per kind)  │
         │    annotations: {...}                (links to external tools)     │
         │    labels: {...}                     (for filtering in the UI)     │
         │    tags: [...]                       (simple string tags)          │
         │  spec:                               ← Type-specific properties    │
         │    type: service                     (varies by kind — see 9.3)    │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.2  Metadata — The Identity Layer
         - name: Unique identifier (MUST be unique per kind)
         - namespace: default (optional, for multi-tenant)
         - title: Display name (can differ from name)
         - description: Human-readable description
         - labels: Key-value pairs for filtering (e.g., language: python)
         - tags: Simple string tags (e.g., [django, backend])
         - annotations: backstage.io/* for integrations
         - links: External URLs (docs, dashboards, repo)
         [METADATA FIELDS — Complete Reference]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  FIELD          REQUIRED?   PURPOSE                                   │
         │  ────────────   ─────────   ──────────────────────────────────────    │
         │  name           ✅ Yes      Unique identifier within kind+namespace    │
         │  namespace      ❌ No       Multi-tenant grouping (defaults to         │
         │                             'default')                                │
         │  title          ❌ No       Human display name (can differ from name)  │
         │  description    ❌ No       Shown on the entity page — always add one  │
         │  labels         ❌ No       key:value pairs for FILTERING in the UI    │
         │  tags           ❌ No       Simple string list for search/browsing     │
         │  annotations    ❌ No       key:value pairs linking to external tools  │
         │  links          ❌ No       External URLs (docs, dashboards, repo)     │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.3  Spec — Type-Specific Properties
         - Component spec: type, lifecycle, owner, system, subcomponentOf,
           providesApis, consumesApis, dependsOn, dependencyOf
         - API spec: type (openapi, asyncapi, graphql, grpc), lifecycle,
           owner, system, definition
         - Resource spec: type (database, s3-bucket, pubsub-topic, etc.),
           owner, system, dependsOn, dependencyOf
         - System spec: owner, domain
         - Domain spec: owner
         - User spec: profile (email, displayName, picture), memberOf
         - Group spec: type (team, squad, tribe, etc.), profile, parent, children
         [SPEC PROPERTIES BY ENTITY KIND]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  KIND        SPEC FIELDS                                             │
         │  ──────────  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────  │
         │  Component   type, lifecycle, owner, system, providesApis,           │
         │              consumesApis, dependsOn, subcomponentOf                 │
         │  API         type, lifecycle, owner, system, definition              │
         │  Resource    type, owner, system, dependsOn, dependencyOf            │
         │  System      owner, domain                                           │
         │  Domain      owner                                                   │
         │  User        profile (email, displayName, picture), memberOf         │
         │  Group       type, profile, parent, children, members                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SPEC EXAMPLES BY KIND — One Real Line Each                                 │
         │                                                                             │
         │  KIND        REAL spec: EXAMPLE                                             │
         │  ----------  ----------------------------------------------------------     │
         │  Component    type: service, lifecycle: production, owner: team-platform    │
         │  API           type: openapi, lifecycle: production, owner: team-platform   │
         │  Resource       type: database, owner: team-platform                        │
         │  System          owner: team-platform, domain: easypj-platform              │
         │  Domain            owner: team-platform                                     │
         │  User                profile: { email: jane@easypj.com }                    │
         │  Group                 type: team, profile: { displayName: Platform Team }  │
         │                                                                             │
         │  Line these up against the field-name table above (9.3) - same fields,      │
         │  now with real easypj values so you can see the shape, not just the names.  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.4  Annotations — The Glue to External Systems
         - backstage.io/managed-by-location: Auto-set
         - backstage.io/managed-by-origin-location: Auto-set
         - backstage.io/view-url: Source code URL
         - backstage.io/edit-url: Edit this entity URL
         - backstage.io/techdocs-ref: TechDocs location
         - github.com/project-slug: GitHub repo
         - github.com/team-slug: GitHub team
         - jenkins.io/job-full-name: Jenkins job
         - pagerduty.com/service-id: PagerDuty service
         - sentry.io/project-slug: Sentry project
         - sonarqube.org/project-key: SonarQube project
         [ALL STANDARD ANNOTATIONS — Quick Reference]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ANNOTATION                              SET BY / MEANING                 │
         │  ───────────────────────────────────     ─────────────────────────────    │
         │  backstage.io/managed-by-location         AUTO-SET — where this entity's  │
         │                                           YAML physically lives           │
         │  backstage.io/managed-by-origin-location  AUTO-SET — original discovery   │
         │                                           source (org scan, etc.)         │
         │  backstage.io/view-url                    YOU set — 'view source' link    │
         │  backstage.io/edit-url                    YOU set — 'edit entity' link    │
         │  backstage.io/techdocs-ref                YOU set — TechDocs location     │
         │  github.com/project-slug                  YOU set — GitHub repo link      │
         │  github.com/team-slug                     YOU set — GitHub team link      │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    9.5  Multi-Entity Files — Using --- Separator
         - Defining multiple entities in one file
         - When to use single vs multi-entity files
         [MULTI-ENTITY catalog-info.yaml — One File, Three Entities]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                                     │
         │  kind: Component                                                       │
         │  metadata: { name: jobpost }                                           │
         │  spec: { type: service, lifecycle: production, owner: team-platform }  │
         │  ---                                                                   │
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                                     │
         │  kind: API                                                             │
         │  metadata: { name: jobpost-api }                                       │
         │  spec: { type: openapi, lifecycle: production, owner: team-platform }  │
         │  ---                                                                   │
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1                                     │
         │  kind: Resource                                                        │
         │  metadata: { name: postgres-db }                                       │
         │  spec: { type: database, owner: team-platform }                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 💡 Use ONE file per entity for large orgs (easier to
         diff in PRs). Use multi-entity files for small, tightly-coupled
         groups you'll always change together, like jobpost + its own API.

    9.6  Validating catalog-info.yaml
         - Using backstage-cli validate
         - Common YAML errors and fixes
         - JSON Schema validation
         [VALIDATING catalog-info.yaml]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # OFFICIAL validation: the Catalog API's ValidateEntity endpoint      │
         │  POST http://localhost:7007/api/catalog/validate-entity                │
         │  Body: the entity YAML (as JSON) — returns 200 OK or 400 + errors      │
         │                                                                        │
         │  # The CLI package is @backstage/cli (NOT @spotify/backstage-cli)      │
         │  npm install -g @backstage/cli                                         │
         │                                                                        │
         │  # Example failure output                                              │
         │  ERROR: data must have required property 'apiVersion'                  │
         │  ERROR: data.spec must have required property 'lifecycle'              │
         │                                                                        │
         │  # Also: Backstage validates on ingestion automatically — check        │
         │  # backend logs after a refresh for the SAME kind of schema errors     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [UNDER THE HOOD 🔧]
         Backstage validates every catalog-info.yaml against a JSON Schema in
         two places: the ValidateEntity API endpoint you can call yourself
         (POST /api/catalog/validate-entity), and automatically again during
         ingestion. Both report the same class of error — most commonly a
         missing required property such as apiVersion or spec.lifecycle — so
         a failure caught locally is the same failure you'd otherwise chase
         through backend logs later (7.7).

    [KEY TAKEAWAYS 🔑]
         🔑 Every catalog-info.yaml needs exactly 4 top-level fields:
            apiVersion, kind, metadata, spec.
         🔑 kind must be one of 9 valid values (9.1).
         🔑 labels are structured key:value pairs for UI filtering; tags are
            plain strings for search; annotations link to external tools —
            don't treat them as interchangeable.
         🔑 name is the only mandatory metadata field; namespace defaults to
            'default'.
         🔑 spec fields differ by kind — Component/API/Resource/System/
            Domain/User/Group each has its own set (9.3).
         🔑 Multi-entity files use a --- separator; prefer one file per
            entity in large orgs for cleaner PR diffs (9.5).
         🔑 Validate with the ValidateEntity API or @backstage/cli — NOT
            @spotify/backstage-cli, which is not the current package.

    [EXAM GOTCHAS ⚠️]
         ⚠️ Forgetting apiVersion — it's always the literal
            backstage.io/v1alpha1.
         ⚠️ Reaching for @spotify/backstage-cli instead of the correct
            @backstage/cli package name.
         ⚠️ Putting a field that belongs to one kind's spec (e.g.
            providesApis) under a different kind (e.g. a Resource).
         ⚠️ Assuming labels and tags are interchangeable — they serve
            different purposes (filtering vs. search).

    [SPEED TIPS ⚡]
         ⚡ Always add a description — it costs nothing and is the first
            thing shown on the entity page.
         ⚡ Run validation locally before committing to catch schema errors
            before they reach ingestion.
         ⚡ Default to one file per entity in large orgs; only combine files
            for tightly-coupled entities you'll always change together.

    [COMMANDS/CONFIG TO REMEMBER 🔑]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  apiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1   # always this exact literal               │
         │  kind: <one of the 9 valid kinds>    # Component/API/System/Domain/            │
         │                                      #  Resource/User/Group/Location/Template  │
         │  metadata: { name, description, annotations, labels, tags, links }             │
         │  spec: { <fields that depend on kind -- see 9.3 table> }                       │
         │                                                                                │
         │  npm install -g @backstage/cli        # NOT @spotify/backstage-cli             │
         │  POST /api/catalog/validate-entity    # validate before/after commit           │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    [PRACTICE DRILL 🧪]
         🧪 In 15 minutes:
         1. Write a minimal valid catalog-info.yaml from memory — just the 4
            required fields (3 min).
         2. Add annotations for GitHub and TechDocs (3 min).
         3. Split one multi-entity file into 3 separate files (4 min).
         4. POST it to /api/catalog/validate-entity and fix any errors
            (5 min).

    9.7  CBA Exam: Chapter 9 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: YAML structure, metadata, spec, annotations, validation

    [VISUAL SUMMARY — Chapter 9: "Anatomy of catalog-info.yaml"]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  catalog-info.yaml                                                         │
         │    apiVersion  ─┐                                                          │
         │    kind        ─┼─▶ 4 REQUIRED top-level fields (9.1)                      │
         │    metadata    ─┤                                                          │
         │    spec        ─┘                                                          │
         │                                                                            │
         │    metadata: name(required) + labels/tags/annotations/links (9.2, 9.4)     │
         │    spec: differs per kind -- see the 9.3 table for each of the 7           │
         │                                                                            │
         │    ---  separates multiple entities in ONE file (9.5)                      │
         │    POST /api/catalog/validate-entity  ──▶  200 OK  or  400 + errors (9.6)  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    .vs-prose — In words: every catalog-info.yaml is built from the same 4
    required top-level fields (9.1), with metadata carrying identity and
    discovery information (9.2, 9.4) and spec carrying whatever fields that
    entity's kind requires (9.3). Multiple entities can share one file with a
    --- separator when they're tightly coupled (9.5), but either way the file
    isn't trustworthy until it passes validation — locally or automatically
    on ingestion (9.6).


Chapter 10: Integrating Kubernetes with the Catalog 🎯 CBA 22% (Catalog)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    [META] 🟡 Intermediate · ⏱️ ~40min · CBA Domain: 22% (Catalog)

    [WHY THIS MATTERS]
         easypj/jobpost's real home isn't a laptop — it's a Kubernetes
         Deployment. Wiring Kubernetes into the Catalog means a developer
         can see jobpost's pod health, service and ingress straight from its
         entity page instead of context-switching to kubectl, closing the
         loop between "what we said we'd run" (Ch9's YAML) and "what's
         actually running."

    [OBJECTIVES — after this chapter you can:]
         ✅ explain what the Kubernetes plugin auto-discovers and displays;
         ✅ configure clusterLocatorMethods (config / catalog / gke /
            localKubectlProxy) in app-config.yaml;
         ✅ link a Deployment to a Catalog entity with kubernetes-id,
            kubernetes-namespace and kubernetes-label-selector;
         ✅ read the Kubernetes tab on an entity page — pods, replica sets,
            services, ingress;
         ✅ containerize easypj/jobpost and register the resulting
            Deployment in the Catalog.

    [CBA RELEVANCE]
         Catalog domain, 22% — this chapter is where the Catalog stops being
         static metadata and starts reflecting live cluster state.

    [SECTION MAP — you are here:]
         10.1 The Kubernetes Plugin -> 10.2 Configuring the Plugin -> 10.3
         Linking K8s Resources -> 10.4 Viewing K8s Resources -> 10.5
         Deploying easypj -> 10.6 Practice Questions.

    10.1 The Kubernetes Plugin for Backstage
         - What the K8s plugin does: Auto-discovers K8s resources
         - Shows: Deployments, Pods, Services, Ingresses in Backstage
         - Real-time status: Pod health, replica counts
         - Links K8s resources to Catalog entities via annotations
         [DIAGRAM: K8s cluster → Backstage Catalog flow]

         [KUBERNETES PLUGIN — Architecture]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────┐            │
         │  │  ☸️ K8s Cluster │───────▶│  Backstage        │            │
         │  │                 │        │  Kubernetes       │            │
         │  │  ┌───────────┐  │        │  Plugin           │            │
         │  │  │ Deployment│  │        │                   │            │
         │  │  │ jobpost   │  │        │  ┌─────────────┐  │            │
         │  │  │ (3 pods)  │  │        │  │    📦       │  │            │
         │  │  └───────────┘  │        │  │ jobpost     │  │            │
         │  │  ┌───────────┐  │        │  │ Component   │  │            │
         │  │  │ Service   │  │        │  │             │  │            │
         │  │  │ jobpost   │  │        │  │  K8s Tab:   │  │            │
         │  │  │ :8000     │  │        │  │  • 3 Pods   │  │            │
         │  │  └───────────┘  │        │  │  • Service  │  │            │
         │  │                 │        │  │  • Ingress  │  │            │
         │  └─────────────────┘        │  └─────────────┘  │            │
