         [CHAPTER 13 VISUAL SUMMARY — 4 templates, 1 shared shape]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  CHAPTER 13 VISUAL SUMMARY — 4 templates, 1 shared shape                  │
         │                                                                           │
         │    django-service (13.1)  react-frontend (13.2)  db-migration-job (13.3)  │
         │    service·team-platform   website·team-fe        service·team-platform   │
         │                                                                           │
         │                         infra-as-code (13.4)                              │
         │                        resource·team-platform                             │
         │                                                                           │
         │                                │                                          │

         │          ALL 4 run:  fetch  ──▶  publish:github  ──▶  catalog:register    │

         .vs-prose — In words: 13.1-13.4 walked through 4 complete, real
         template.yaml files — a Django microservice, a React frontend, a
         database migration job, and a Terraform infrastructure module —
         each with its own type, owner, and parameters. Despite scaffolding
         very different things, all 4 run the identical fetch → publish:github
         → catalog:register step sequence from Chapters 11-12. 13.5 then
         showed how all 4 get switched on at once: one catalog.locations
         entry per template, each gated by rules: [{allow: [Template]}], in
         the same app-config.yaml that registers every other easypj entity.


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
  🔑 PART 4 — MUST-REMEMBER
   • A Software Template = template.yaml (metadata + spec) + a skeleton/
     directory of files with {{ values.X }} placeholders + a sequence of steps.
   • The near-universal 3-step shape is fetch → publish → register: copy the
     skeleton, push it to GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Azure, register the result
     in the Catalog — only the skeleton/ contents differ between templates.
   • Built-in actions (fetch:*, publish:*, catalog:*, fs:delete,
     roadiehq:utils:fs:replace) cover the vast majority of templates; write a
     custom action with createTemplateAction only when nothing built-in fits.
   • Every step's OUTPUT becomes available to every LATER step as
     ${{ steps.<id>.output.<field> }} — that is how register can reach back to
     what publish produced without either action knowing the other exists.
   • spec.output.links/text controls what the user actually sees on the
     scaffolder "Task" completion page — skip it and they see only raw logs.
   • easypj ships exactly 4 templates today — django-service (service),
     react-frontend (website), db-migration-job (service), infra-as-code
     (resource) — each registered as a type: file catalog.locations entry
     with rules: [{allow: [Template]}] in app-config.yaml.
   • Templates are registered in the Catalog exactly like any other entity —
     which is why Part 3 (the Catalog) had to come before Part 4 (Templates).

  [PART 4 VISUAL SUMMARY — From blank form to 4 live easypj templates]
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  PART 4 VISUAL SUMMARY — From blank form to 4 live easypj templates  │
  │                                                                      │
  │    👤 Dev clicks CREATE ──▶ picks one of 4 templates ──▶ fills form  │
  │             (Ch11: template.yaml + parameters define this form)      │
  │                                                                      │
  │                              │                                       │
  │                              ▼                                       │
  │   ⚙️  STEPS RUN IN ORDER (Ch12: built-in + custom actions)           │
  │     fetch:template ──▶ [optional custom action] ──▶ publish:github   │
  │     ──▶ catalog:register                                             │
  │                                                                      │
  │                              │                                       │
  │                              ▼                                       │
  │   📦 NEW ENTITY LIVE IN CATALOG (Ch13: 1 of the 4 real templates)    │
  │     django-service · react-frontend · db-migration-job ·             │
  │     infra-as-code                                                    │
  │                                                                      │
  │   ⏱️  ~2 minutes, end to end — versus ~2 hours scaffolding by hand   │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  .vs-prose — In words: a developer clicks Create, picks one of easypj's 4
  templates, and fills in a form generated straight from that template's
  parameters (Chapter 11). Backstage then runs the template's steps in order —
  almost always fetch:template to copy the skeleton, optionally a custom
  action for anything org-specific, then publish:github and catalog:register
  (Chapter 12). Seconds later the new service — a Django microservice, a React
  frontend, a DB migration job, or a Terraform module (Chapter 13) — is a live,
  searchable entity in the same Software Catalog Part 3 introduced. What took
  a developer roughly 2 hours by hand now takes about 2 minutes, end to end.

  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────


PART 5: TECHDOCS — DOCUMENTATION-AS-CODE                                  🟡
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

Chapter 14: TechDocs — Documentation That Lives With Your Code 🎯 CBA 32% (Customizing)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14.1 What are TechDocs?
         - Documentation-as-code: Docs live in your repo, next to code
         - Written in Markdown (.md) or MDX (.mdx)
         - Auto-published when you push to Git
         - Rendered beautifully in Backstage
         - Powered by MkDocs under the hood
         [DIAGRAM: Code + Docs together in Git → TechDocs → Backstage]

         [TECHDOCS PIPELINE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  📖 TECHDOCS — Documentation-as-Code Pipeline                │
         │                                                              │
         │  📝 Developer writes docs in Markdown                         │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  📦 Docs live in same repo as code                           │
         │     /docs/                                                   │
         │       index.md                                               │
         │       architecture.md                                        │
         │       api-reference.md                                       │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🚀 Git push triggers TechDocs build                         │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  ⚙️ TechDocs Generator (MkDocs)                              │
         │     • Converts Markdown → Static HTML                        │
         │     • Applies MkDocs theme                                   │
         │     • Generates navigation                                   │
         │     • Creates search index                                   │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  📤 TechDocs Publisher                                       │
         │     • Publishes to storage (local, GCS, S3, Azure)           │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🖥️ Backstage TechDocs Reader                                 │
         │     • Displays docs on Entity page                           │
         │     • Full-text search                                       │
         │     • Navigation between docs                                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [TECHDOCS ARCHITECTURE — Official Component Names]
         - Generator: runs MkDocs to convert Markdown into static HTML/CSS/JS, stripping the JS afterward for security before handing the output to the Publisher
         - Publisher: stores the generated static files — local filesystem in a Basic setup, or cloud storage (AWS S3, GCS, Azure Blob Storage) in a Recommended/production setup
         - techdocs-backend plugin: the single backend intermediary that retrieves generated docs from storage and serves them, centralizing security handling across every storage type
         - Reader: the frontend piece that requests docs from techdocs-backend using the entity ID and renders them on the entity page
         - Backstage names these two deployment models 'Basic' (docs generated on-demand on the Backstage server itself, stored locally) and 'Recommended' (docs generated in CI/CD, stored in cloud storage, served on-demand by techdocs-backend)

    14.2 Setting Up TechDocs
         - mkdocs.yml configuration
         - docs/ directory structure
         - Markdown best practices for TechDocs
         - Adding diagrams (Mermaid, PlantUML)
         - Adding code snippets with syntax highlighting
         [CODE BLOCK: Complete mkdocs.yml example]

         [mkdocs.yml — TechDocs Configuration]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # mkdocs.yml                                                │
         │  site_name: 'jobpost Documentation'                          │
         │  site_description: 'Job posting application docs'            │
         │                                                              │
         │  nav:                                                        │
         │    - Home: index.md                                          │
         │    - Architecture: architecture.md                           │
         │    - 'API Reference': api-reference.md                       │
         │    - 'Getting Started': getting-started.md                   │
         │    - Deployment: deployment.md                               │
         │    - Contributing: contributing.md                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  plugins:                                                    │
         │    - techdocs-core                                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  markdown_extensions:                                        │
         │    - admonition                                              │
         │    - codehilite                                              │
         │    - pymdownx.superfences:                                   │
         │        custom_fences:                                        │
         │          - name: mermaid                                     │
         │            class: mermaid                                    │
         │            format: !!python/name:pymdownx.superfences.       │
         │                    fence_div_format                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  docs/ DIRECTORY ANATOMY — Generic MkDocs Project Layout                     │
         │                                                                              │
         │  jobpost/                     (repo root)                                    │
         │    mkdocs.yml                  <- nav + plugins config (shown above)         │
         │    docs/                                                                     │
         │      index.md                   <- required, the home page                   │
         │      architecture.md                                                         │
         │      api-reference.md                                                        │
         │      assets/                      <- images referenced by ![](assets/x.png)  │
         │      overrides/                     <- optional, custom theme overrides      │
         │                                                                              │
         │  Only docs/ + mkdocs.yml are required - assets/ and overrides/ are opt-in.   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    14.3 TechDocs Configuration in app-config.yaml
         - techdocs.builder: local | external
         - techdocs.generator: runIn (local | docker)

         - techdocs.generator.dockerImage: overrides the default MkDocs Docker image used when runIn: docker
         - techdocs.generator.pullImage: boolean controlling whether a fresh copy of the Docker image is pulled before each build
         - techdocs.generator.mkdocs.omitTechdocsCorePlugin: boolean (default false) — skips auto-injecting the techdocs-core plugin into mkdocs.yml
         - techdocs.generator.mkdocs.legacyCopyReadmeMdToIndexMd: boolean fallback that copies README.md to index.md when docs/index.md is missing
         - techdocs.generator.mkdocs.disableExternalFonts: boolean for air-gapped networks that can't reach external font CDNs
         - techdocs.generator.mkdocs.defaultPlugins: array of MkDocs plugin names injected by default
         - techdocs.publisher: local | googleGcs | awsS3 | azureBlobStorage
         - Storage configuration for production
         [TECHDOCS SECTION — app-config.yaml, Dev vs Production]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Development                                                  │
         │  techdocs:                                                      │
         │    builder: local                                               │
         │    generator: { runIn: local }                                  │
         │    publisher: { type: local }                                   │
         │                                                                 │
         │  # Production                                                   │
         │  techdocs:                                                      │
         │    builder: external          # CI/CD pre-builds docs           │
         │    generator: { runIn: docker } # isolated MkDocs container     │
         │    publisher:                                                   │
         │      type: awsS3                                                │
         │      awsS3: { bucketName: easypj-techdocs, region: us-east-1 }  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - googleGcs needs publisher.googleGcs.bucketName (required); optional: bucketRootPath, projectId, credentials
         - azureBlobStorage needs publisher.azureBlobStorage.containerName (required — note the field is containerName, not bucketName); optional: connectionString, credentials
         - awsS3 also accepts optional bucketRootPath, endpoint, s3ForcePathStyle, and sse ('aws:kms' or 'AES256') for server-side encryption

    14.4 Writing Effective TechDocs
         - Structure: index.md as entry point
         - Navigation: mkdocs.yml nav section
         - Admonitions: Notes, warnings, tips
         - Code blocks with line highlighting
         - Mermaid diagrams for architecture
         - Cross-references between docs
         [CODE BLOCK: Example TechDocs MD file with best practices]

         [TECHDOCS STORAGE STRATEGIES — Dev vs Production]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  💾 TECHDOCS PUBLISHER — Storage Options                     │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ PUBLISHER TYPE     DEV          PRODUCTION              ││
         │  │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ local              ✅ Default   ❌ Not scalable          ││
         │  │                    Stores in    Multiple pods can't     ││
         │  │                    ./techdocs-  share local storage     ││
         │  │                    output/                               ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ googleGcs          🟡 Config    ✅ Recommended           ││
         │  │ (Google Cloud      needed       Auto-scales, cheap,     ││
         │  │  Storage)                       CDN-ready               ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ awsS3              🟡 Config    ✅ Recommended           ││
         │  │ (AWS S3)           needed       Auto-scales, CDN via    ││
         │  │                                 CloudFront              ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ azureBlobStorage   🟡 Config    ✅ Recommended           ││
         │  │ (Azure Blob)       needed       Auto-scales, CDN-ready  ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ openStackSwift     🟡 Config    ✅ For OpenStack shops   ││
         │  │                    needed                                ││
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 For production: ALWAYS use cloud storage (GCS/S3/Azure) │
         │     for multi-pod Backstage deployments!                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [TECHDOCS WRITING BEST PRACTICES — Do's and Don'ts]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ✍️ TECHDOCS WRITING GUIDE                                    │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ DO:                          ❌ DON'T:                    │
         │  ─────────────────────────────   ──────────────────────────  │
         │  ✅ Keep docs next to code       ❌ Put docs in a separate   │
         │     (/docs/ in each repo)           wiki/Confluence          │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Use Mermaid for diagrams     ❌ Use screenshots that     │
         │     (text-based, versionable)       go stale quickly         │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Structure with clear nav     ❌ One giant README with    │
         │     (mkdocs.yml nav section)        everything               │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Use admonitions for          ❌ Bury important warnings  │
         │     notes/warnings/tips             in paragraphs            │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Include code snippets        ❌ Describe code without    │
         │     with syntax highlighting        showing it               │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Write for NEW developers     ❌ Assume everyone knows    │
         │     (explain acronyms, context)     the context              │
         │                                                              │
         │  ✅ Update docs WITH code        ❌ "I'll update docs later" │
         │     (same PR as code change)        (never happens)          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    15.2 Writing Architecture Documentation
         - System overview with Mermaid diagrams
         - Component relationships
         - Data flow diagrams
         - Deployment architecture
         [architecture.md — Mermaid System Diagram]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # jobpost Architecture                                             │
         │                                                                     │
         │  ```mermaid                                                         │
         │  graph TD                                                           │
         │      A[Browser] --> B[Nginx Ingress]                                │
         │      B --> C[jobpost Service :8000]                                 │
         │      C --> D[(PostgreSQL)]                                          │
         │      C --> E[(Redis Cache)]                                         │
         │  ```                                                                │
         │                                                                     │
         │  jobpost is a Django service exposing a REST API for job postings,  │
         │  backed by PostgreSQL for storage and Redis for query caching.      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MERMAID DIAGRAM TYPES WORTH KNOWING FOR ARCHITECTURE DOCS                             │
         │                                                                                        │
         │  TYPE               USE FOR                        ONE-LINE EXAMPLE                    │
         │  -----------------  -----------------------------  --------------------------------    │
         │  graph TD             System/component diagrams       A[Browser] --> B[Service]        │
         │                       (shown above for jobpost)                                        │
         │  sequenceDiagram       Request/response timing          Client->>API: GET /jobs        │
         │                        between services                  API-->>Client: 200 OK         │
         │  erDiagram               Database schema relationships     JOBPOST ||--o{ SKILL : has  │
         │                                                                                        │
         │  All three render as live, versionable diagrams straight from Markdown - no            │
         │  screenshot to keep updating by hand every time the architecture changes.              │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    15.3 Writing API Documentation
         - REST API endpoints for jobpost
         - Request/response examples
         - Authentication and error codes
         [api-reference.md — Real Endpoint Documentation]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ## GET /api/jobs/                                                 │
         │  Returns a paginated list of job postings.                         │
         │                                                                    │
         │  **Query params:** page, skills, location                          │
         │                                                                    │
         │  **Response (200):**                                               │
         │  ```json                                                           │
         │  { "count": 42, "results": [                                       │
         │    { "id": 1, "title": "Senior Developer", "skills": ["Python"] }  │
         │  ]}                                                                │
         │  ```                                                               │
         │  **Auth:** Bearer token required in the Authorization header.      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    15.4 Writing Deployment Documentation
         - Kubernetes deployment guide
         - Environment variables
         - Database setup
         - CI/CD pipeline guide
         [deployment.md — Real Deployment Guide]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Deploying jobpost                                      │
         │                                                           │
         │  ## Environment Variables                                 │
         │  | Variable | Required | Example |                        │
         │  |----------|----------|---------|                        │
         │  | DATABASE_URL | Yes | postgresql://user:pass@host/db |  │
         │  | DJANGO_SECRET_KEY | Yes | (openssl rand -hex 32) |     │
         │                                                           │
         │  ## Deploy                                                │
         │  kubectl apply -f k8s/deployment.yaml                     │
         │  kubectl apply -f k8s/service.yaml                        │
         │                                                           │
         │  ## Rollback                                              │
         │  kubectl rollout undo deployment/jobpost                  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    15.5 Publishing and Viewing TechDocs
         - Registering easypj in the Catalog with TechDocs annotation
         - Verifying TechDocs appear on the entity page
         - Updating docs: Push to Git → auto-rebuild
         [PUBLISHING TECHDOCS — Complete Workflow]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. Add annotation to catalog-info.yaml:                            │
         │     backstage.io/techdocs-ref: dir:.                                │
         │  2. Create mkdocs.yml at repo root (nav + techdocs-core plugin)     │
         │  3. Write the 6 docs from 15.1 into /docs/                          │
         │  4. git push — TechDocs builds automatically                        │
         │  5. Visit the jobpost entity page → click the Docs tab              │
         │  6. Future doc updates: same PR as the code change → auto-rebuild   │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Local preview without a running Backstage instance: `npx @techdocs/cli serve` builds mkdocs.yml with MkDocs and serves the rendered site with live reload, so docs can be checked before pushing
         - `techdocs-cli` is the same official CLI package used by CI/CD pipelines to generate and publish docs straight to cloud storage under the 'external' builder model

    15.6 CBA Exam: Chapter 15 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Complete TechDocs setup, writing, publishing

Chapter 16: Plugin Architecture 🎯 CBA 32% (Customizing)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16.1 What are Backstage Plugins?
         - Plugins are the building blocks of Backstage
         - Every feature is a plugin (Catalog, TechDocs, Scaffolder, Search)
         - Plugins can be: Frontend-only, Backend-only, or Full-stack
         - Plugin ecosystem: 200+ community plugins
         - You can build custom plugins for YOUR organization

         - ℹ️ Official docs now label createPlugin/createRoutableExtension
           (shown in 16.2) as the LEGACY frontend system. Newer Backstage
           apps build frontend plugins with createFrontendPlugin() from
           @backstage/frontend-plugin-api instead — same underlying
           concepts (plugin id, routable extensions, ApiRefs) but a
           different API surface
         [DIAGRAM: Plugin architecture overview]

         [PLUGIN ARCHITECTURE — How Plugins Work]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
         │  │              BACKSTAGE APP                           │    │
         │  │                                                     │    │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐          │    │
         │  │  │ Catalog  │  │Scaffolder│  │ TechDocs │  ...     │    │
         │  │  │ Plugin   │  │ Plugin   │  │ Plugin   │          │    │
         │  │  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘          │    │
         │  │       │             │              │                │    │
         │  │  ┌────┴─────────────┴──────────────┴────┐          │    │
         │  │  │         PLUGIN FRAMEWORK              │          │    │
         │  │  │  • Plugin Registry                    │          │    │
         │  │  │  • Route Management                   │          │    │
         │  │  │  • API Client Proxy                   │          │    │
         │  │  │  • Component Mounting                 │          │    │
         │  │  │  • Event Bus                          │          │    │
         │  │  └──────────────────────────────────────┘          │    │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
         │                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.2 Frontend Plugins
         - createPlugin() API
         - createRoutableExtension() for routing
         - createComponentExtension() for cards
         - Plugin API: useApi(), createApiRef()

         - createApiFactory() ties a createApiRef() definition to a concrete
           implementation (registered in packages/app/src/apis.ts) — the
           ApiRef alone is just an id/type, the factory supplies the object
           useApi() actually returns
         - Accessing backend data from frontend
         [FRONTEND PLUGIN SKELETON — Real Code]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard/src/plugin.ts                          │
         │  import { createPlugin, createRoutableExtension } from              │
         │    '@backstage/core-plugin-api';                                    │
         │  import { rootRouteRef } from './routes';                           │
         │                                                                     │
         │  export const easypjDashboardPlugin = createPlugin({                │
         │    id: 'easypj-dashboard',                                          │
         │    routes: { root: rootRouteRef },                                  │
         │  });                                                                │
         │                                                                     │
         │  export const EasypjDashboardPage = easypjDashboardPlugin.provide(  │
         │    createRoutableExtension({                                        │
         │      name: 'EasypjDashboardPage',                                   │
         │      component: () => import('./components/DashboardPage')          │
         │        .then(m => m.DashboardPage),                                 │
         │      mountPoint: rootRouteRef,                                      │
         │    }),                                                              │
         │  );                                                                 │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.3 Backend Plugins
         - createRouter() for HTTP endpoints
         - Database access via PluginDatabaseManager
         - URL reading via UrlReader
         - Event system for cross-plugin communication
         - Scheduling tasks (cron-like)

         - ⚠️ LEGACY NOTE: The createRouter()/@backstage/backend-common
           pattern shown below is the OLD backend system. The official docs
           confirm @backstage/backend-common is now deprecated — new backend
           plugins should use createBackendPlugin() from
           @backstage/backend-plugin-api instead (the New Backend System,
           built on 5 concepts: Backend, Plugins, Services, Extension
           Points, and Modules). See 18.3 for the modern pattern.
         [BACKEND PLUGIN SKELETON — Real Code]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard-backend/src/service/router.ts              │
         │  import { createRouter } from '@backstage/backend-common';              │
         │  import express from 'express';                                         │
         │                                                                         │
         │  export async function createEasypjRouter(): Promise<express.Router> {  │
         │    const router = express.Router();                                     │
         │    router.use(express.json());                                          │
         │                                                                         │
         │    router.get('/job-stats', async (_req, res) => {                      │
         │      res.json({ activePostings: 42, newToday: 3 });                     │
         │    });                                                                  │
         │                                                                         │
         │    return router;                                                       │
         │  }                                                                      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    16.4 The Plugin Marketplace
         - @backstage/plugin-catalog
         - @backstage/plugin-scaffolder
         - @backstage/plugin-techdocs
         - @backstage/plugin-search
         - @backstage/plugin-kubernetes
         - Community plugins: PagerDuty, Sentry, Jira, Grafana, etc.

         │                                                              │
         │  💡 200+ plugins available at backstage.io/plugins           │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [FRONTEND vs BACKEND vs FULL-STACK PLUGINS]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔌 PLUGIN TYPES — When to Use Each                          │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ TYPE          DESCRIPTION        EXAMPLE                ││
         │  │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ 🖥️ Frontend   Only renders UI.    • GitHub README card  ││
         │  │    Only       Calls external APIs  • Grafana iframe      ││
         │  │               directly from       • TechDocs reader      ││
         │  │               browser.                                  ││
         │  │                                                         ││
         │  │ ⚙️ Backend    Only runs on        • Catalog processors   ││
         │  │    Only       server. Processes   • Search indexing      ││
         │  │               data, no UI.        • Auth providers       ││
         │  │                                                         ││
         │  │ 🔄 Full-Stack Has BOTH frontend   • Kubernetes plugin    ││
         │  │               AND backend.        • Scaffolder           ││
         │  │               Most common type.   • PagerDuty            ││
         │  │                                    • Sentry               ││
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 Most plugins are Full-Stack: UI in packages/app,         │
         │     backend logic in packages/backend                        │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 17: Essential Plugins for Daily Use 🎯 CBA 32% (Customizing)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WHERE DO ALL THESE PLUGINS SHOW UP? — jobpost Entity Page Map                │
         │                                                                               │
         │  jobpost ENTITY PAGE                                                          │
         │    |-- Overview tab                                                           │
         │    |     |-- GitHub card (README, contributors, latest PRs)        17.1       │
         │    |     |-- PagerDuty card (on-call, open incidents)              17.4       │
         │    |-- CI/CD tab                                                              │
         │    |     |-- GitHub Actions status                                17.2        │
         │    |     |-- Jenkins build status                                 17.2        │
         │    |     |-- ArgoCD sync/health status                            17.2, 21.4  │
         │    |-- Monitoring tab                                                         │
         │    |     |-- Grafana embedded dashboard                           17.3        │
         │    |     |-- Sentry recent errors card                            17.5        │
         │    |-- (no tab — background notifications)                                    │
         │          |-- Slack: scaffolder/deploy alerts to #easypj-deploys   17.6        │
         │                                                                               │
         │  Every card on this map comes from ONE annotation on catalog-info.yaml -      │
         │  the plugin reads the annotation, the entity page renders the card.           │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.1 GitHub Integration Plugin
         - Installing and configuring
         - Viewing repo details, README, contributors
         - GitHub Actions CI/CD status
         - Pull request overview

         - Feature discovery: if app.packages is set to 'all' in
           app-config.yaml (the default for new Backstage apps), plugins
           added as app dependencies — including these integration plugins —
           are auto-detected and installed with no App.tsx code changes;
           otherwise they're wired manually via createApp's features array
         [GITHUB PLUGIN CONFIGURATION]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                          │
         │  integrations:                              │
         │    github:                                  │
         │      - host: github.com                     │
         │        token: ${GITHUB_TOKEN}               │
         │                                             │
         │  # Annotation needed on the entity          │
         │  # github.com/project-slug: easypj/jobpost  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.2 CI/CD Plugins (Jenkins, GitHub Actions, ArgoCD)
         - Jenkins plugin: View builds, trigger jobs
         - GitHub Actions: Workflow status
         - ArgoCD plugin: GitOps deployment status
         [CI/CD PLUGIN CONFIGURATION — GitHub Actions + ArgoCD]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Annotations enable each CI/CD plugin's card:                          │
         │  metadata:                                                               │
         │    annotations:                                                          │
         │      github.com/project-slug: easypj/jobpost      # GitHub Actions card  │
         │      jenkins.io/job-full-name: easypj/jobpost-build # Jenkins card       │
         │      argocd/app-name: jobpost                        # ArgoCD card       │
         │                                                                          │
         │  # app-config.yaml — ArgoCD backend needs its API endpoint               │
         │  argocd:                                                                 │
         │    baseUrl: https://argocd.easypj.com                                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.3 Monitoring Plugins (Grafana, Prometheus, Datadog)
         - Grafana: Embed dashboards
         - Prometheus: Alert status
         - Datadog: Service health
         [MONITORING PLUGIN CONFIGURATION — Grafana]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                                   │
         │  proxy:                                              │
         │    endpoints:                                        │
         │      '/grafana/api':                                 │
         │        target: https://grafana.easypj.com            │
         │        headers: { Authorization: ${GRAFANA_TOKEN} }  │
         │                                                      │
         │  # Annotation on the entity                          │
         │  # grafana/dashboard-selector: 'tag:jobpost'         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.4 Incident Management (PagerDuty, OpsGenie)
         - PagerDuty: On-call schedule, incident status
         - Linking services to incident management
         [PAGERDUTY PLUGIN CONFIGURATION]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                     │
         │  pagerDuty:                            │
         │    apiToken: ${PAGERDUTY_TOKEN}        │
         │                                        │
         │  # Annotation on the entity            │
         │  metadata:                             │
         │    annotations:                        │
         │      pagerduty.com/service-id: P12345  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.5 Error Tracking (Sentry, Rollbar)
         - Sentry: Error overview, recent issues
         - Linking projects to Sentry
         [SENTRY PLUGIN CONFIGURATION]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                           │
         │  sentry:                                     │
         │    organization: easypj                      │
         │                                              │
         │  # Annotation on the entity                  │
         │  metadata:                                   │
         │    annotations:                              │
         │      sentry.io/project-slug: easypj/jobpost  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    17.6 Communication (Slack, Microsoft Teams)
         - Slack plugin: Channel integration
         - Teams plugin: Notifications
         [COMMUNICATION PLUGIN CONFIGURATION — Slack]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                                                 │
         │  slack:                                                            │
         │    webhookUrl: ${SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL}                                │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Used by Scaffolder notifications and custom actions (see 12.3)  │
         │  # to post to a #easypj-deploys channel on template completion     │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Chapter 18: Building Custom Plugins
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    18.1 When to Build a Custom Plugin
         - No existing plugin for your tool
         - Custom internal workflows
         - Organization-specific dashboards
         - Data aggregation from multiple sources
         [DECISION TREE — Build a Custom Plugin, or Not?]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Does an official/community plugin already do this?                     │
         │      |                                                                  │
         │      |-- YES -> Use it. Check backstage.io/plugins first, always.       │
         │      |                                                                  │
         │      |-- NO -> Is this workflow SPECIFIC to your org (not reusable      │
         │              by other Backstage users)?                                 │
         │              |                                                          │
         │              |-- YES -> Build a custom plugin (18.2-18.4)               │
         │              |                                                          │
         │              |-- NO -> Consider open-sourcing it as a community plugin  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.2 Creating a Frontend Plugin — Step by Step
         - yarn new (interactive), or backstage-cli new --select frontend-plugin
         - Plugin structure: index.ts, PluginPage.tsx, api.ts
         - Registering routes and navigation
         - Adding plugin to the sidebar
         - Styling with Material UI
         [COMPLETE WALKTHROUGH with code]

         [CUSTOM PLUGIN — Directory Structure]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  plugins/easypj-dashboard/                                   │
         │  ├── src/                                                    │
         │  │   ├── index.ts              ← Plugin entry, exports       │
         │  │   ├── plugin.ts             ← createPlugin() definition   │
         │  │   ├── api.ts                ← API client (if needed)      │
         │  │   ├── routes.ts             ← Route definitions           │
         │  │   └── components/                                         │
         │  │       ├── EasypjPage.tsx     ← Main plugin page           │
         │  │       ├── JobStatsCard.tsx   ← Dashboard card             │
         │  │       └── ServiceHealth.tsx  ← Health status component    │
         │  ├── package.json                                            │
         │  └── README.md                                               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.3 Creating a Backend Plugin
         - yarn new (interactive), or backstage-cli new --select backend-plugin
         - Creating API endpoints with Express Router
         - Database access for plugin data
         - Authentication and authorization in plugins
         [BACKEND PLUGIN WALKTHROUGH — Wiring the Router]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard-backend/src/plugin.ts                     │
         │  import { createBackendPlugin } from '@backstage/backend-plugin-api';  │
         │  import { createEasypjRouter } from './service/router';                │
         │                                                                        │
         │  export const easypjDashboardBackendPlugin = createBackendPlugin({     │
         │    pluginId: 'easypj-dashboard',                                       │
         │    register(env) {                                                     │
         │      env.registerInit({                                                │
         │        deps: { httpRouter: coreServices.httpRouter },                  │
         │        async init({ httpRouter }) {                                    │
         │          httpRouter.use(await createEasypjRouter());                   │
         │        },                                                              │
         │      });                                                               │
         │    },                                                                  │
         │  });                                                                   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.4 Building the easypj Dashboard Plugin
         - Purpose: Custom dashboard showing jobpost metrics
         - Features: Job stats, deployment status, recent errors
         - Frontend: React components with MUI
         - Backend: API endpoints for metrics
         [easypj DASHBOARD PLUGIN — Frontend Calling the Backend]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard/src/components/DashboardPage.tsx                    │
         │  import { useApi, discoveryApiRef } from '@backstage/core-plugin-api';           │
         │  import { InfoCard, Progress } from '@backstage/core-components';                │
         │  import useAsync from 'react-use/lib/useAsync';                                  │
         │                                                                                  │
         │  export const DashboardPage = () => {                                            │
         │    const discoveryApi = useApi(discoveryApiRef);                                 │
         │    const { value, loading } = useAsync(async () => {                             │
         │      const baseUrl = await discoveryApi.getBaseUrl('easypj-dashboard');          │
         │      const res = await fetch(baseUrl + '/job-stats');                            │
         │      return res.json();                                                          │
         │    });                                                                           │
         │    if (loading) return <Progress />;                                             │
         │    return <InfoCard title='Job Stats'>{value.activePostings} active</InfoCard>;  │
         │  };                                                                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.5 Testing Custom Plugins
         - Unit tests with Jest
         - Integration tests
         - Manual testing with yarn dev
         [TESTING CUSTOM PLUGINS — Real Test Code]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard/src/components/DashboardPage.test.tsx    │
         │  import { renderInTestApp } from '@backstage/test-utils';             │
         │  import { DashboardPage } from './DashboardPage';                     │
         │                                                                       │
         │  describe('DashboardPage', () => {                                    │
         │    it('renders job stats', async () => {                              │
         │      const { getByText } = await renderInTestApp(<DashboardPage />);  │
         │      expect(getByText(/active/i)).toBeInTheDocument();                │
         │    });                                                                │
         │  });                                                                  │
         │                                                                       │
         │  # Run: yarn test --testPathPattern=easypj-dashboard                  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    18.6  Making Changes to React Code in the Backstage App
         - This is DIFFERENT from building a plugin (18.1-18.4): here you edit
           the App SHELL itself — packages/app/src/App.tsx and Root.tsx
         - Common App-shell changes: add a route, add/remove a sidebar item,
           change the homepage, wire up a custom theme (see Ch4.5)
         - App.tsx wires plugins + routes together; Root.tsx defines the
           persistent shell (sidebar, header) that wraps every page

         [APP SHELL FILES — What Lives Where]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  FILE                          WHAT YOU EDIT IT FOR                       │
         │  ────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────────────────────  │
         │  packages/app/src/App.tsx       Register plugins/APIs, define <Routes>,   │
         │                                 set the homepage component                │
         │  packages/app/src/Root.tsx      Sidebar items, header, persistent layout  │
         │  packages/app/src/apis.ts       Wire up ApiRef implementations            │
         │  packages/app/src/components/    Your own shared React components used    │
         │                                 across multiple pages                     │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ADDING A NEW ROUTE — Real Diff on App.tsx]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/app/src/App.tsx                                               │
         │  import { EasypjDashboardPage } from '@internal/plugin-easypj-dashboard';  │
         │                                                                            │
         │  const routes = (                                                          │
         │    <FlatRoutes>                                                            │
         │      <Route path='/catalog' element={<CatalogIndexPage />} />              │
         │      <Route path='/create' element={<ScaffolderPage />} />                 │
         │  +   <Route path='/easypj-dashboard' element={<EasypjDashboardPage />} />  │
         │    </FlatRoutes>                                                           │
         │  );                                                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [ADDING A SIDEBAR ITEM — Real Diff on Root.tsx]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/app/src/components/Root/Root.tsx                                │
         │  import DashboardIcon from '@material-ui/icons/Dashboard';                   │
         │                                                                              │
         │  export const Root = ({ children }: PropsWithChildren<{}>) => (              │
         │    <SidebarPage>                                                             │
         │      <Sidebar>                                                               │
         │        <SidebarItem icon={HomeIcon} to='catalog' text='Home' />              │
         │        <SidebarItem icon={CreateComponentIcon} to='create' text='Create' />  │
         │  +     <SidebarItem icon={DashboardIcon} to='easypj-dashboard'               │
         │  +                  text='easypj Dashboard' />                               │
         │      </Sidebar>                                                              │
         │      {children}                                                              │
         │    </SidebarPage>                                                            │
         │  );                                                                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [CHANGING THE HOMEPAGE]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/app/src/App.tsx                                              │
         │  const routes = (                                                         │
         │    <FlatRoutes>                                                           │
         │      <Navigate key='/' to='catalog' />        // default: Catalog page    │
         │  +   <Navigate key='/' to='easypj-dashboard' /> // custom: your own page  │
         │    </FlatRoutes>                                                          │
         │  );                                                                       │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 🔑 CBA distinction: building a PLUGIN (18.1-18.4) creates
         reusable, installable code in its own package. Editing App.tsx/Root.tsx
         directly is ORG-SPECIFIC wiring — it can't be reused by another
         Backstage instance the way a real plugin package can.

    18.7  Using Material UI Components
         - Backstage's entire UI is built on Material UI (MUI v4/v5 — check
           your version, Backstage is migrating between the two)
         - You do NOT need a new CSS framework to build plugin UI — MUI
           components + Backstage's own Core Components cover almost
           everything you'll need

         [COMMON MUI COMPONENTS IN BACKSTAGE PLUGINS]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  COMPONENT        IMPORT FROM                    USE IT FOR                      │
         │  ───────────────  ──────────────────────────     ────────────────────────        │
         │  Typography        @material-ui/core               All text — h1-h6, body1       │
         │  Button             @material-ui/core               Actions, form submits        │
         │  Card / CardContent @material-ui/core               Dashboard panels             │
         │  Grid                @material-ui/core               Responsive layout           │
         │  Table / TableRow     @material-ui/core               Tabular data (job list)    │
         │  TextField             @material-ui/core               Form inputs               │
         │  Chip                    @material-ui/core               Tags, status badges     │
         │  CircularProgress         @material-ui/core               Loading states         │
         │  InfoCard                  @backstage/core-components     Backstage-styled card  │
         │  Table (Backstage's)        @backstage/core-components     Sortable/filterable   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [REAL CODE — A JobStatsCard Using MUI + Backstage Components]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // plugins/easypj-dashboard/src/components/JobStatsCard.tsx       │
         │  import { InfoCard } from '@backstage/core-components';            │
         │  import { Grid, Typography, Chip } from '@material-ui/core';       │
         │                                                                    │
         │  export const JobStatsCard = () => (                               │
         │    <InfoCard title='Job Postings'>                                 │
         │      <Grid container spacing={2}>                                  │
         │        <Grid item xs={6}>                                          │
         │          <Typography variant='h4'>42</Typography>                  │
         │          <Typography variant='body2'>Active postings</Typography>  │
         │        </Grid>                                                     │
         │        <Grid item xs={6}>                                          │
         │          <Chip label='3 new today' color='primary' />              │
         │        </Grid>                                                     │
         │      </Grid>                                                       │
         │    </InfoCard>                                                     │
         │  );                                                                │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [STYLING — sx PROP vs makeStyles]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  MUI v5 (sx prop — preferred, newer)     MUI v4 (makeStyles — legacy)      │
         │  ──────────────────────────────────      ──────────────────────────────    │
         │  <Box sx={{ p: 2, bgcolor: 'primary.',    const useStyles = makeStyles({   │
         │                   main' }}>                   root: { padding: 16 },       │
         │    Content                                 });                             │
         │  </Box>                                    const classes = useStyles();    │
         │                                            <div className={classes.root}>  │
         │  No separate hook needed — styles          Requires a hook + className —   │
         │  live INLINE on the component               more boilerplate, but still    │
         │                                             widely used in Backstage core  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [INFO BOX] 🎯 CBA Tip — Theming: Backstage themes (Ch4.5) are built
         with MUI's createTheme(). Any component using MUI's theme-aware
         props (palette.primary.main, spacing()) automatically adopts a
         custom theme — you rarely need to hardcode colors in a plugin.

    18.8 CBA Exam: Chapter 18 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Custom plugin creation, structure, testing, App.tsx/Root.tsx
           editing, and Material UI component usage
