Chapter 19: Deploying Backstage on Kubernetes
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19.1 Why Deploy Backstage on Kubernetes?
         - High availability: Multiple replicas
         - Auto-scaling: Handle traffic spikes
         - Self-healing: Pod crashes auto-restart
         - GitOps: Everything declared in YAML
         - Same platform your services run on
         [BENEFITS CARD: Why K8s for Backstage]

         [BACKSTAGE ON KUBERNETES — Production Architecture]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🏗️ BACKSTAGE PRODUCTION K8s ARCHITECTURE                    │
         │                                                              │
         │                        🌐 INTERNET                           │
         │                           │                                  │
         │                    ┌──────┴──────┐                           │
         │                    │  ☁️  CDN    │ ← Static assets           │
         │                    └──────┬──────┘                           │
         │                           │                                  │
         │                    ┌──────┴──────┐                           │
         │                    │  🔀 Ingress │ ← TLS termination         │
         │                    │  (nginx)    │    backstage.easypj.com   │
         │                    └──────┬──────┘                           │
         │                           │                                  │
         │              ┌────────────┴────────────┐                     │
         │              │    ☸️ KUBERNETES CLUSTER │                     │
         │              │                         │                     │
         │  ┌───────────┴─────────────────────────┴───────────┐        │
         │  │              backstage Namespace                 │        │
         │  │                                                 │        │
         │  │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐   │        │
         │  │  │         Backstage Service (ClusterIP)    │   │        │
         │  │  │              port: 7007                  │   │        │
         │  │  └────────────────┬────────────────────────┘   │        │
         │  │                   │                             │        │
         │  │     ┌─────────────┼─────────────┐              │        │
         │  │     │             │             │              │        │
         │  │     ▼             ▼             ▼              │        │
         │  │  ┌──────┐    ┌──────┐    ┌──────┐             │        │
         │  │  │ Pod 1│    │ Pod 2│    │ Pod 3│ ← 3 replicas│        │
         │  │  │ 🖥️   │    │ 🖥️   │    │ 🖥️   │             │        │
         │  │  │ 7007 │    │ 7007 │    │ 7007 │             │        │
         │  │  └──┬───┘    └──┬───┘    └──┬───┘             │        │
         │  │     │           │           │                  │        │
         │  │     └───────────┼───────────┘                  │        │
         │  │                 │                              │        │
         │  │     ┌───────────┴───────────┐                  │        │
         │  │     │   PostgreSQL Service   │                  │        │
         │  │     │     (ClusterIP)        │                  │        │
         │  │     └───────────┬───────────┘                  │        │
         │  │                 │                              │        │
         │  │     ┌───────────┴───────────┐                  │        │
         │  │     │   PostgreSQL Pod       │                  │        │
         │  │     │   💾 /data (PVC)       │                  │        │
         │  │     └───────────────────────┘                  │        │
         │  │                                                 │        │
         │  │  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐       │        │
         │  │  │ConfigMap │ │  Secret  │ │   PVC    │       │        │
         │  │  │app-config│ │ DB creds │ │TechDocs  │       │        │
         │  │  │  .yaml   │ │GH token  │ │ cache    │       │        │
         │  │  └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘       │        │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘        │
         │                                                              │
         │  External Dependencies:                                      │
         │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐                  │
         │  │  GitHub  │  │   Okta   │  │  Google  │                  │
         │  │   API    │  │   SSO    │  │ Cloud K8s│                  │
         │  └──────────┘  └──────────┘  └──────────┘                  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [REAL-LIFE: Deploying Backstage to K8s — Exact Commands]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # 1. Build and push Docker image                             │
         │  $ docker build -t registry.easypj.com/backstage:v1.0 .      │
         │  $ docker push registry.easypj.com/backstage:v1.0             │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 2. Create namespace and secrets                            │
         │  $ kubectl create namespace backstage                        │
         │  $ kubectl create secret generic backstage-secrets \         │
         │      --from-literal=POSTGRES_HOST=postgres.easypj.com \     │
         │      --from-literal=GITHUB_TOKEN=ghp_xxxx \                 │
         │      -n backstage                                           │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 3. Create ConfigMap from app-config.yaml                  │
         │  $ kubectl create configmap backstage-config \               │
         │      --from-file=app-config.yaml -n backstage                │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 4. Apply deployment and service                            │
         │  $ kubectl apply -f k8s/deployment.yaml                      │
         │  $ kubectl apply -f k8s/service.yaml                         │
         │  $ kubectl apply -f k8s/ingress.yaml                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 5. Verify deployment                                       │
         │  $ kubectl get pods -n backstage -w    # Watch pods start    │
         │  $ kubectl logs deploy/backstage -n backstage                │
         │  $ curl https://backstage.easypj.com/healthcheck             │
         │                                                              │
         │  # 6. Scale up for production                                 │
         │  $ kubectl scale deploy/backstage --replicas=3 -n backstage  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.2 Backstage Docker Image
         - Official Dockerfile from Backstage
         - Building a custom Docker image for easypj-portal
         - Multi-stage builds for optimization
         - Image tagging strategy
         [CODE BLOCK: Dockerfile for Backstage]

         [DOCKERFILE — Backstage on K8s]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Dockerfile for Backstage with easypj customization        │
         │                                                              │
         │  FROM node:18-bookworm-slim AS build                         │
         │                                                              │
         │  WORKDIR /app                                                │
         │                                                              │
         │  COPY package.json yarn.lock ./                              │
         │  RUN yarn install --frozen-lockfile --production=false       │
         │                                                              │
         │  COPY . .                                                    │
         │  RUN yarn tsc                                               │
         │  RUN yarn build                                              │
         │                                                              │
         │  FROM node:18-bookworm-slim AS runtime                       │
         │                                                              │
         │  WORKDIR /app                                                │
         │                                                              │
         │  COPY --from=build /app/packages/backend/dist ./dist         │
         │  COPY --from=build /app/packages/backend/package.json ./     │
         │  COPY --from=build /app/node_modules ./node_modules          │
         │                                                              │
         │  ENV NODE_ENV=production                                     │
         │  EXPOSE 7007                                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  CMD ["node", "packages/backend", "--config", "app-config.yaml"]│
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.3 Kubernetes Manifests for Backstage
         - Namespace: backstage
         - Deployment: 3 replicas, resource limits
         - Service: ClusterIP + Ingress
         - ConfigMap: app-config.yaml
         - Critical fields inside that mounted app-config.yaml: app.baseUrl, backend.baseUrl, backend.listen.port, and backend.cors.origin must all resolve to the externally-reachable URL (e.g. the Ingress host), or the frontend cannot reach the backend
         - Secret: Database credentials, GitHub tokens
         - PersistentVolumeClaim: For TechDocs cache
         [COMPLETE K8s MANIFESTS — All YAML files]

         [K8s DEPLOYMENT — Backstage on Kubernetes]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # backstage-deployment.yaml                                 │
         │  apiVersion: apps/v1                                         │
         │  kind: Deployment                                            │
         │  metadata:                                                   │
         │    name: backstage                                           │
         │    namespace: backstage                                      │
         │  spec:                                                       │
         │    replicas: 3                                               │
         │    selector:                                                 │
         │      matchLabels:                                            │
         │        app: backstage                                        │
         │    template:                                                 │
         │      metadata:                                               │
         │        labels:                                               │
         │          app: backstage                                      │
         │      spec:                                                   │
         │        containers:                                           │
         │        - name: backstage                                     │
         │          image: easypj/backstage:latest                      │
         │          ports:                                              │
         │            - containerPort: 7007                             │
         │          env:                                                │
         │            - name: POSTGRES_HOST                             │
         │              valueFrom:                                      │
         │                secretKeyRef:                                 │
         │                  name: backstage-secrets                     │
         │                  key: postgres-host                          │
         │            - name: GITHUB_TOKEN                              │
         │              valueFrom:                                      │
         │                secretKeyRef:                                 │
         │                  name: backstage-secrets                     │
         │                  key: github-token                           │
         │          volumeMounts:                                       │
         │            - name: config                                    │
         │              mountPath: /app/app-config.yaml                 │
         │              subPath: app-config.yaml                        │
         │          resources:                                          │
         │            requests:                                         │
         │              memory: "512Mi"                                 │
         │              cpu: "500m"                                     │
         │            limits:                                           │
         │              memory: "2Gi"                                   │
         │              cpu: "2000m"                                    │
         │        volumes:                                              │
         │          - name: config                                      │
         │            configMap:                                        │
         │              name: backstage-config                          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.4 Helm Chart for Backstage
         - Official Backstage Helm chart
         - Official FAQ caveat: Backstage's own docs explain there are no official published Helm charts or Docker images because "Backstage is not a packaged service that you can use out of the box" - every real deployment is built from your own customized `@backstage/create-app` output, so treat the community backstage/charts chart as a starting point, not a drop-in production artifact
         - Customizing values.yaml
         - Installing with Helm
         - Upgrading with Helm
         [HELM CHART — Install, Upgrade, Rollback]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ helm repo add backstage https://backstage.github.io/charts  │
         │  $ helm repo update                                            │
         │                                                                │
         │  $ helm install backstage backstage/backstage \                │
         │      --namespace backstage --create-namespace \                │
         │      --set appConfig.app.title='easypj Developer Portal' \     │
         │      --set database.client=pg \                                │
         │      --set ingress.enabled=true \                              │
         │      --set ingress.host=backstage.easypj.com                   │
         │                                                                │
         │  # Upgrade after editing values.yaml                           │
         │  $ helm upgrade backstage backstage/backstage -n backstage     │
         │                                                                │
         │  # Rollback to the previous release                            │
         │  $ helm rollback backstage -n backstage                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    19.5 CBA Exam: Chapter 19 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Docker, K8s deployment, Helm, configuration


Chapter 20: Kubernetes Plugin Deep Dive for Backstage
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    20.1 Architecture of the Kubernetes Plugin
         - Frontend: Kubernetes tab on entity pages
         - Backend: KubernetesServiceApi communicating with K8s API
         - Shipped as two npm packages: frontend `@backstage/plugin-kubernetes`, backend `@backstage/plugin-kubernetes-backend`
         - How it discovers resources by annotations
         - Cluster locator methods: config, gke, catalog, localKubectlProxy (eks/aks are authProvider values for per-cluster auth, not locator method types)
         - Service locator methods: multiTenant, singleTenant, catalogRelation
         [K8s PLUGIN ARCHITECTURE — Frontend + Backend]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  Entity Page -> "KUBERNETES" tab (frontend)                             │
         │      |                                                                  │
         │      v  REST call: /api/kubernetes/services/:entityRef                  │
         │  KubernetesServiceApi (backend)                                         │
         │      |-- reads entity annotations (kubernetes-id, namespace, selector)  │
         │      |-- calls the K8s API server (get/list/watch)                      │
         │      |-- merges results across ALL configured clusters                  │
         │      v                                                                  │
         │  Returned to the frontend tab as Deployments/Pods/Services/Ingresses    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  HOW kubernetes-id RESOLVES TO REAL PODS                                                 │
         │                                                                                          │
         │  catalog-info.yaml                          Running in the cluster                       │
         │    metadata:                                    Deployment "jobpost"                     │
         │      annotations:                                 labels:                                │
         │        backstage.io/kubernetes-id: jobpost -->      backstage.io/kubernetes-id: jobpost  │
         │                                                                                          │
         │  The plugin does NOT match on entity name - it matches ONLY on this                      │
         │  annotation-to-label pair. Forget the label on the Deployment and the                    │
         │  Kubernetes tab stays empty even with a perfectly correct annotation.                    │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.2 Multi-Cluster Setup
         - Adding multiple K8s clusters to Backstage
         - Cluster authentication per cluster
         - Dashboard URL per cluster
         - Custom cluster naming
         - Single-cluster mode: set serviceLocatorMethod to singleTenant and tag the entity with the backstage.io/kubernetes-cluster annotation to pin it to one specific cluster
         [MULTI-CLUSTER CONFIGURATION]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  kubernetes:                                          │
         │    serviceLocatorMethod: { type: multiTenant }        │
         │    clusterLocatorMethods:                             │
         │      - type: config                                   │
         │        clusters:                                      │
         │          - name: easypj-prod                          │
         │            url: https://k8s-prod.easypj.com           │
         │            authProvider: serviceAccount               │
         │            serviceAccountToken: ${K8S_PROD_TOKEN}     │
         │          - name: easypj-staging                       │
         │            url: https://k8s-staging.easypj.com        │
         │            authProvider: serviceAccount               │
         │            serviceAccountToken: ${K8S_STAGING_TOKEN}  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.3 Custom Kubernetes Dashboards
         - Building a custom K8s overview card
         - Filtering resources by namespace/labels
         - Displaying pod metrics (CPU, memory)
         [CUSTOM K8s DASHBOARD — Real Code]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // A custom entity page card showing pod CPU/memory                       │
         │  import { useApi, kubernetesApiRef } from '@backstage/plugin-kubernetes';  │
         │  import { InfoCard } from '@backstage/core-components';                    │
         │                                                                            │
         │  export const PodMetricsCard = () => {                                     │
         │    const kubernetesApi = useApi(kubernetesApiRef);                         │
         │    // fetch pod objects, then render CPU/memory with MUI                   │
         │    return <InfoCard title='Pod Metrics'>...</InfoCard>;                    │
         │  };                                                                        │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.4 Troubleshooting K8s Plugin Issues
         - Common errors: RBAC, connectivity, TLS
         - Debugging service account permissions
         - Checking plugin logs
         [K8s PLUGIN — Common Errors and Solutions]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ERROR                CAUSE                       FIX                           │
         │  -------------------  --------------------------  -------------------------     │
         │  403 Forbidden          ServiceAccount lacks RBAC    Grant get/list/watch on    │
         │                                                     Deployments/Pods/Services   │
         │  Connection refused      Wrong API server URL         Verify cluster URL        │
         │  TLS certificate error    Self-signed cert not trusted  skipTLSVerify (dev) or  │
         │                                                        add CA cert (prod)       │
         │  No resources shown        Missing kubernetes-id        Add the annotation,     │
         │                          annotation                    match Deployment name    │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.5 RBAC for the Kubernetes Plugin ServiceAccount
         - The plugin only needs READ access, never write
         - Least-privilege ClusterRole for the Backstage ServiceAccount
         - Scoping to specific namespaces instead of cluster-wide
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # ClusterRole - read-only, exactly what the K8s plugin needs                                            │
         │  apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1                                                                │
         │  kind: ClusterRole                                                                                       │
         │  metadata: { name: backstage-k8s-plugin-reader }                                                         │
         │  rules:                                                                                                  │
         │    - apiGroups: [""]                                                                                     │
         │      resources: ["pods", "services", "configmaps", "limitranges"]                                        │
         │      verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]                                                                     │
         │    - apiGroups: ["apps"]                                                                                 │
         │      resources: ["deployments", "replicasets"]                                                           │
         │      verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]                                                                     │
         │    - apiGroups: ["networking.k8s.io"]                                                                    │
         │      resources: ["ingresses"]                                                                            │
         │      verbs: ["get", "list", "watch"]                                                                     │
         │  ---                                                                                                     │
         │  # Bind it to the ServiceAccount Backstage authenticates as                                              │
         │  apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1                                                                │
         │  kind: ClusterRoleBinding                                                                                │
         │  metadata: { name: backstage-k8s-plugin-binding }                                                        │
         │  roleRef: { kind: ClusterRole, name: backstage-k8s-plugin-reader, apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io }  │
         │  subjects:                                                                                               │
         │    - kind: ServiceAccount                                                                                │
         │      name: backstage                                                                                     │
         │      namespace: backstage                                                                                │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.6 Kubernetes Plugin vs Full GitOps Tools
         - The K8s plugin is a READ-ONLY window, not a deploy mechanism
         - Knowing when a view is enough and when you need ArgoCD (Ch 21)
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  NEED                              K8s PLUGIN ALONE       ADD ArgoCD / GitOps (Ch 21)            │
         │  ---------------------------------  ----------------------  ------------------------------       │
         │  See pod/deployment status           YES - built for this    Also shown, via sync status         │
         │  See recent rollout history           Limited                YES - full revision history         │
         │  Trigger a deployment                  NO - view only         YES - sync button / auto-sync      │
         │  Roll back a bad release                NO                     YES - one click, git revert       │
         │  Enforce Git as source of truth          NO                     YES - that is the whole point    │
         │  See CPU/memory of running pods           YES (Ch 20.3)          Complementary, not overlapping  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    20.7 CBA Exam: Chapter 20 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: K8s plugin architecture, multi-cluster, troubleshooting


Chapter 21: GitOps with Backstage and ArgoCD
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    21.1 What is GitOps?
         - Git as the single source of truth
         - Declarative infrastructure
         - Automated reconciliation
         - ArgoCD, Flux, and how they fit
         [GITOPS OVERVIEW — Push vs Pull]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  PUSH (old way):              PULL / GitOps (modern way):                     │
         │  CI/CD -- kubectl apply -->    Git Repo <-- polls every ~3min -- ArgoCD/Flux  │
         │  K8s Cluster                                                     |            │
         │  (needs cluster credentials     ArgoCD/Flux runs INSIDE the cluster           │
         │   in CI - security risk)         and pulls changes - more secure              │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.2 Backstage + ArgoCD Integration
         - ArgoCD plugin: View application status
         - Auto-register ArgoCD apps in Backstage
         - Deploy from Backstage via ArgoCD
         [ARGOCD PLUGIN CONFIGURATION]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                                             │
         │  argocd:                                                       │
         │    baseUrl: https://argocd.easypj.com                          │
         │    username: ${ARGOCD_USERNAME}                                │
         │    password: ${ARGOCD_PASSWORD}                                │
         │                                                                │
         │  # Annotation on the entity links it to an ArgoCD Application  │
         │  metadata:                                                     │
         │    annotations:                                                │
         │      argocd/app-name: jobpost                                  │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  WHAT THE ARGOCD CARD ACTUALLY LOOKS LIKE — jobpost Entity Page                  │
         │                                                                                  │
         │  ArgoCD Application: jobpost                                                     │
         │  Sync Status:   Synced       (green)                                             │
         │  Health Status: Healthy      (green)                                             │
         │  Last Sync:     2 minutes ago                                                    │
         │  Revision:      a1b2c3d (main)                                                   │
         │  [Open in ArgoCD]   [Sync Now]   [View History]                                  │
         │                                                                                  │
         │  Both badges come straight from the ArgoCD API via the baseUrl/username/         │
         │  password configured above - Backstage never talks to Kubernetes directly here.  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.3 GitOps Workflow for easypj
         - Code push → CI builds image → Updates K8s manifests → ArgoCD syncs
         - Entire pipeline visible in Backstage
         - Rollback: Git revert → ArgoCD auto-syncs
         [COMPLETE GITOPS WORKFLOW — Code to Production]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. Developer pushes code to jobpost repo                               │
         │  2. GitHub Actions builds Docker image, pushes to registry              │
         │  3. CI updates the image tag in the k8s-manifests repo                  │
         │  4. ArgoCD detects the Git change (polls every ~3 min)                  │
         │  5. ArgoCD syncs the new image to the K8s cluster                       │
         │  6. Backstage entity page shows: CI green, ArgoCD Synced, K8s 3/3 pods  │
         │                                                                         │
         │  ROLLBACK: git revert the manifest change → ArgoCD auto-syncs back      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.4 ArgoCD Application Health and Sync States
         - Two independent axes: SYNC status and HEALTH status
         - What Backstage displays for each combination
         - Which combinations mean trouble
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │                    HEALTHY            PROGRESSING         DEGRADED                  │
         │  Synced          All good, done      Rollout in flight   Deployed but crashing      │
         │                   (green in UI)       (blue, wait)         (red - investigate NOW)  │
         │                                                                                     │
         │  OutOfSync       Manual drift        New commit landed    Bad manifest pushed,      │
         │                   exists in cluster    mid-rollout           pods failing to start  │
         │                   (yellow - review)    (blue, will sync)     (red - fix + re-sync)  │
         │                                                                                     │
         │  Backstage entity page mirrors these two badges straight from the ArgoCD API,       │
         │  so "Synced + Healthy" is the ONLY combination that means fully done and safe.      │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.5 Flux vs ArgoCD — Choosing a GitOps Engine
         - Both implement the same pull-based GitOps model (21.1)
         - Backstage has first-class plugins for both
         - Picking one is an org decision, not a Backstage limitation
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ASPECT                ArgoCD                          Flux                                 │
         │  ---------------------  ------------------------------  ------------------------------      │
         │  UI                      Rich built-in web UI            CLI-first, no bundled UI           │
         │  Backstage integration     @roadiehq/backstage-plugin-    @weaveworksoss techdocs +         │
         │                             argo-cd                         Flux annotations                │
         │  Multi-tenancy               AppProjects                    Kustomization + Flagger         │
         │  Progressive delivery          Argo Rollouts (separate)      Flagger (separate)             │
         │  Best fit                        Teams that want a visual      Teams already deep in        │
         │                                    dashboard non-experts use     Kubernetes-native tooling  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.6 Common GitOps Pitfalls at easypj
         - Mistakes teams make once ArgoCD is wired into Backstage
         - How each one shows up on the entity page
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  PITFALL                          HOW IT SHOWS UP            FIX                                      │
         │  --------------------------------  --------------------------  ------------------------------         │
         │  kubectl apply run by hand           OutOfSync forever,          Never kubectl apply directly;        │
         │                                       fights the next auto-sync   always commit to the repo           │
         │  Image tag "latest" in manifests      ArgoCD sees no diff,        Use immutable tags (git SHA)        │
         │                                        never redeploys new code    so every change is a real diff     │
         │  Secrets committed in plaintext         Security scanner fails,    Use Sealed Secrets or External     │
         │                                          drifted from the vault      Secrets Operator (Ch 30.2)       │
         │  Two repos out of sync (app vs infra)     CI updates one, forgets    Automate the manifest-repo bump  │
         │                                            the other                  as part of the CI job (Ch 25)   │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    21.7 CBA Exam: Chapter 21 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: GitOps concepts, ArgoCD integration, workflows

Chapter 22: Authentication Providers 🎯 CBA 22% (Infrastructure)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    22.1 Authentication in Backstage
         - Sign-in flow: How users authenticate
         - Provider types: GitHub, GitLab, Google, Okta, Azure AD, etc.
         - JWT tokens and session management
         - Configuring auth in app-config.yaml
         [AUTH FLOW DIAGRAM]

         [AUTHENTICATION FLOW]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔐 AUTHENTICATION FLOW                                      │
         │                                                              │
         │  👤 User clicks "Sign In"                                    │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🔀 Redirect to Auth Provider (GitHub/Okta/Google)           │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  ✅ User authenticates with provider                         │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🎫 Provider returns token to Backstage                      │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🔑 Backstage creates session (JWT)                          │
         │      │                                                       │
         │      ▼                                                       │
         │  🖥️ User is logged in!                                       │
         │     • Identity from provider (name, email, avatar)           │
         │     • Mapped to Backstage User entity                        │
         │     • Group memberships synced                               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [AUTH PROVIDER COMPARISON — Which One for Your Org?]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🔐 AUTH PROVIDER COMPARISON                                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐│
         │  │ PROVIDER     TYPE      BEST FOR         SETUP EFFORT    ││
         │  │─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ GitHub       OAuth2    Dev teams on     🟢 Easy         ││
         │  │                        GitHub. Auto-    Create OAuth    ││
         │  │                        maps teams→      App in GitHub   ││
         │  │                        Backstage Groups                 ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ GitLab       OAuth2    Dev teams on     🟢 Easy         ││
         │  │                        GitLab. Similar  Create OAuth    ││
         │  │                        to GitHub auth   App in GitLab   ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Google       OAuth2    GSuite/Google    🟢 Easy         ││
         │  │                        Workspace orgs.  Google Cloud    ││
         │  │                        Quick setup      Console config  ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Okta         OIDC/     Enterprise with  🟡 Medium       ││
         │  │              SAML      existing Okta.   Configure app   ││
         │  │                        SSO + group sync in Okta admin   ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Azure AD     OIDC/     Microsoft 365/   🟡 Medium       ││
         │  │ (Entra ID)   SAML      Azure shops.     App registration││
         │  │                        Group sync via   in Azure portal ││
         │  │                        claims                          ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Microsoft    OIDC/     Microsoft 365    🟡 Medium       ││
         │  │              SAML      (without Azure)                  ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Auth0        OIDC      Flexible,         🟡 Medium       ││
         │  │                        multi-provider   Configure in    ││
         │  │                        identity hub     Auth0 dashboard ││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Keycloak     OIDC/     Self-hosted,     🔴 Complex      ││
         │  │              SAML      open-source      Deploy & config ││
         │  │                        identity          Keycloak server││
         │  ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────││
         │  │ Guest        None      Development      🟢 None         ││
         │  │ (No Auth)              only! NEVER      Just enable in  ││
         │  │                        in production    app-config      ││
         │  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘│
         │                                                              │
         │  💡 RECOMMENDATION: Start with GitHub auth for dev teams,   │
         │     Okta/Azure AD for enterprise. NEVER use guest in prod!  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         [CORRECTION: Okta/Azure AD Rows Say "OIDC/SAML" — Built-Ins Are OIDC-Only]
         The table above lists Okta and Azure AD (and Microsoft/Keycloak) as
         "OIDC/SAML". Per the official docs, Backstage's built-in okta and
         microsoft auth providers are OAuth2/OIDC only — there is no built-in
         named SAML provider. Backstage instead ships a generic authentication
         provider for OAuth2 and SAML that reduces the code needed to build a
         *custom* provider adhering to those standards. If your Okta or Azure
         AD tenant is set up for SAML rather than OIDC, you need to build a
         custom auth provider on top of the generic SAML provider rather than
         using the okta/microsoft config blocks shown in 22.3.

         [REAL-LIFE: Setting Up GitHub OAuth for Backstage]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STEP 1: Create GitHub OAuth App (github.com → Settings →    │
         │  Developer settings → OAuth Apps → New OAuth App)            │
         │  • Application name: easypj-backstage-dev                    │
         │  • Homepage URL: http://localhost:3000                       │
         │  • Callback URL: http://localhost:7007/api/auth/github/      │
         │    handler/frame                                              │
         │  • Copy Client ID and Client Secret                          │
         │                                                              │
         │  STEP 2: Configure app-config.yaml                           │
         │  auth:                                                       │
         │    environment: development                                   │
         │    providers:                                                 │
         │      github:                                                  │
         │        development:                                           │
         │          clientId: ${GITHUB_CLIENT_ID}  # ← From env var     │
         │          clientSecret: ${GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET}               │
         │                                                              │
         │  STEP 3: Set env vars and restart                             │
         │  $ export GITHUB_CLIENT_ID=abc123                            │
         │  $ export GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET=xyz789                        │
         │  $ yarn dev                                                  │
         │                                                              │
         │  STEP 4: Test — Open http://localhost:3000                   │
         │  Click "Sign In" → "GitHub" → Authorize → You're logged in!  │
         │  Your GitHub username/avatar appears in the top right.       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Creating a GitHub OAuth App
         - Configuring GitHub auth in Backstage
         - GitHub Teams → Backstage Groups mapping
         - Sign-in resolver: How GitHub user becomes Backstage User
         [COMPLETE GITHUB AUTH SETUP - Sign-In Resolver]
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml                                        │
         │  auth:                                                    │
         │    environment: production                                │
         │    providers:                                             │
         │      github:                                              │
         │        production:                                        │
         │          clientId: ${GITHUB_CLIENT_ID}                    │
         │          clientSecret: ${GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET}            │
         │          signIn:                                          │
         │            resolvers:                                     │
         │              - resolver: usernameMatchingUserEntityName   │
         │                                                           │
         │  # This resolver maps the GitHub username to an existing  │
         │  # User entity with the same metadata.name                │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    22.3 Okta / Azure AD (Enterprise SSO)
         - SAML/OIDC configuration
         - Group claim mapping
         - Enterprise sign-in flow
         [OKTA / AZURE AD CONFIGURATION]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Okta                                        │
         │  auth:                                         │
         │    providers:                                  │
         │      okta:                                     │
         │        production:                             │
         │          clientId: ${OKTA_CLIENT_ID}           │
         │          clientSecret: ${OKTA_CLIENT_SECRET}   │
         │          audience: https://easypj.okta.com     │
         │                                                │
         │  # Azure AD (Entra ID)                         │
         │  auth:                                         │
         │    providers:                                  │
         │      microsoft:                                │
         │        production:                             │
         │          clientId: ${AZURE_CLIENT_ID}          │
         │          clientSecret: ${AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET}  │
         │          tenantId: ${AZURE_TENANT_ID}          │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    22.4 Multiple Auth Providers
         - Configuring multiple providers simultaneously
         - Provider selection UI
         - Sign-in resolver customization
         [MULTIPLE AUTH PROVIDERS CONFIGURATION]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  auth:                                                             │
         │    environment: production                                         │
         │    providers:                                                      │
         │      github: { production: { clientId: ..., clientSecret: ... } }  │
         │      okta:   { production: { clientId: ..., clientSecret: ... } }  │
         │                                                                    │
         │  # Users see a "Sign in with..." picker showing every configured   │
         │  # provider. Each provider can have its own sign-in resolver.      │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    22.5 Guest/Anonymous Access
         - When to allow unauthenticated access
         - Limiting what guests can see
         [GUEST ACCESS CONFIGURATION - Dev Only]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # app-config.yaml (DEV ONLY - never in production)              │
         │  auth:                                                           │
         │    environment: development                                      │
         │    providers:                                                    │
         │      guest: {}                                                   │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # Guests get a fixed "guest" identity with no real permissions  │
         │  # scoping - this is why it must NEVER be enabled in production  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         - Q1-Q10: Auth providers, configuration, sign-in flow


Chapter 23: Permissions & RBAC 🎯 CBA 22% (Infrastructure)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    23.1 The Backstage Permission Framework
         - Permission policies: Allow/Deny rules
         - Resource types: catalog-entity, catalog-entity-refresh, etc.
         - Actions: read, create, update, delete
         - Conditions: isEntityOwner, hasAnnotation, hasLabel, etc.
         - Every permission check resolves to one of three AuthorizeResult
           values: ALLOW, DENY, or CONDITIONAL. CONDITIONAL is returned when
           the policy can't yet tell if a request should be allowed (e.g. it
           depends on whether the current user owns a specific entity) — the
           condition travels with the response so the caller can apply it
           once the actual resource is known, which is what lets list/read
           endpoints filter results instead of allowing or denying outright.
         [PERMISSION FRAMEWORK DIAGRAM]

         [PERMISSION FRAMEWORK]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  🛡️ BACKSTAGE PERMISSION SYSTEM                              │
         │                                                              │
         │  ┌─────────────────┐                                         │
         │  │  Permission     │  ← Define what actions exist            │
         │  │  Policy         │    and who can do them                  │
         │  └────────┬────────┘                                         │
         │           │                                                  │
         │           ▼                                                  │
         │  ┌─────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────┐                 │
         │  │  Allow Rules    │     │  Deny Rules     │                 │
         │  │  "If owner,     │     │  "If not in     │                 │
         │  │   allow delete" │     │   group X, deny"│                 │
         │  └────────┬────────┘     └────────┬────────┘                 │
         │           │                        │                         │
         │           └────────┬───────────────┘                         │
         │                    ▼                                          │
         │  ┌─────────────────────────────────┐                         │
         │  │       Permission Evaluator      │                         │
         │  │  Checks each request against    │                         │
         │  │  the policy rules               │                         │
         │  └─────────────────────────────────┘                         │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.2 Configuring Permission Policies
         - @backstage/plugin-permission-backend
         - Policy file: packages/backend/src/plugins/permission.ts
         - Built-in conditions reference
         - Combining rules with AND/OR logic
         [CODE BLOCK: Complete permission policy example]

         [PERMISSION POLICY EXAMPLE]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/backend/src/extensions/permissionsPolicyExtension.ts        │
         │                                                                          │
         │  import { AuthorizeResult } from '@backstage/plugin-permission-common';  │
         │  import {                                                                │
         │    catalogConditions,                                                    │
         │    createCatalogConditionalDecision,                                     │
         │  } from '@backstage/plugin-catalog-backend/alpha';                       │
         │  import { catalogEntityDeletePermission } from                           │
         │    '@backstage/plugin-catalog-common/alpha';                             │
         │  import { PermissionPolicy, PolicyQuery, PolicyQueryUser }               │
         │    from '@backstage/plugin-permission-node';                             │
         │                                                                          │
         │  class MyPermissionPolicy implements PermissionPolicy {                  │
         │    async handle(request: PolicyQuery, user?: PolicyQueryUser) {          │
         │      if (request.permission.name ===                                     │
         │          catalogEntityDeletePermission.name) {                           │
         │        return createCatalogConditionalDecision(                          │
         │          request.permission,                                             │
         │          catalogConditions.isEntityOwner({                               │
         │            claims: user?.info.ownershipEntityRefs ?? [],                 │
         │          }),                                                             │
         │        );                                                                │
         │      }                                                                   │
         │      return { result: AuthorizeResult.ALLOW };                           │
         │    }                                                                     │
         │  }                                                                       │
         │                                                                          │
         │  // Registered via policyExtensionPoint in                               │
         │  // packages/backend/src/extensions/permissionsPolicyExtension.ts,       │
         │  // added with backend.add(import('./extensions/                         │
         │  // permissionsPolicyExtension')) in packages/backend/src/index.ts       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.3 Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)
         - Defining roles: admin, developer, viewer
         - Mapping Backstage Groups to roles
         - Permission inheritance
         [RBAC CONFIGURATION - Roles via Groups]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/backend/src/plugins/permission.ts                      │
         │  const roleForGroup = {                                             │
         │    'group:default/team-platform-admins': 'admin',                   │
         │    'group:default/team-platform': 'developer',                      │
         │  };                                                                 │
         │                                                                     │
         │  // admin role: full catalog + scaffolder access                    │
         │  // developer role: read catalog, run templates, edit own entities  │
         │  // viewer role (default): read-only across the board               │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.4 Conditional Permissions
         - isEntityOwner: Only the entity's owner
         - hasAnnotation: Check for specific annotations
         - hasLabel: Check for specific labels
         - hasSpec: Check spec fields
         - Custom conditions
         [ALL BUILT-IN PERMISSION CONDITIONS]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  RULE (catalogConditions.*)   CHECKS IF...                                     │
         │  ------------------------------  --------------------------------------------  │
         │  isEntityOwner                   User/Group is the entity owner                │
         │  isEntityKind                     Entity is a specific kind (e.g. Component)   │
         │  hasAnnotation                    Entity has a specific annotation             │
         │  hasLabel                         Entity has a specific label                  │
         │  hasSpec                          Entity has a specific spec field/value       │
         │  hasMetadata                      Entity has a specific metadata field/value   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.5 Testing Permissions
         - Using the permission evaluator
         - Testing as different users
         - Debugging permission denials
         [TESTING PERMISSIONS - Commands]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Check what a specific user can do                             │
         │  curl -X POST localhost:7007/api/permission/authorize \          │
         │    -H "Authorization: Bearer $USER_TOKEN" \                      │
         │    -d {"items":[{"permission":"catalog.entity.delete",           │
         │                  "resourceRef":"component:default/jobpost"}]}    │
         │                                                                  │
         │  # Response: { result: "ALLOW" } or { result: "DENY" }           │
         │  # Debugging denials: check backend logs for which rule matched  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    23.6 CBA Exam: Chapter 23 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Permission framework, RBAC, conditions, policies


Chapter 24: Integrating with External Identity Providers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    24.1 LDAP / Active Directory Integration
         - Catalog ingestion from LDAP
         - User and Group auto-discovery
         - Syncing schedules
         [LDAP INTEGRATION CONFIGURATION]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  catalog:                                                            │
         │    providers:                                                        │
         │      ldapOrg:                                                        │
         │        default:                                                      │
         │          target: ldaps://ldap.easypj.com                             │
         │          bind: { dn: ${LDAP_BIND_DN}, secret: ${LDAP_BIND_SECRET} }  │
         │          users:                                                      │
         │            - dn: ou=people,dc=easypj,dc=com                          │
         │              options: { filter: (objectClass=person) }               │
         │          groups:                                                     │
         │            - dn: ou=groups,dc=easypj,dc=com                          │
         │          schedule: { frequency: PT30M, timeout: PT5M }               │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  LDAP TO BACKSTAGE ENTITY RESOLUTION                                     │
         │                                                                          │
         │  LDAP directory (ou=people,dc=easypj,dc=com)                             │
         │    entry: uid=jdoe, cn=Jane Doe, mail=jane@easypj.com                    │
         │                              │  matched by the users.dn + filter (24.1)  │
         │                              ▼                                           │
         │  Ingestion runs on schedule (every 30 min, configured above)             │
         │                              │                                           │
         │                              ▼                                           │
         │  Backstage User entity created/updated:                                  │
         │    kind: User, metadata.name: jdoe, spec.profile.email: jane@easypj.com  │
         │                                                                          │
         │  Groups resolve the same way, one level up, from ou=groups (24.1).       │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.2 Okta / Azure AD Group Sync
         - Syncing groups from Okta/Azure AD
         - Mapping to Backstage Group entities
         - Automatic updates on group changes
         [OKTA GROUP SYNC CONFIGURATION]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  catalog:                                                   │
         │    providers:                                               │
         │      okta:                                                  │
         │        default:                                             │
         │          orgUrl: https://easypj.okta.com                    │
         │          token: ${OKTA_API_TOKEN}                           │
         │          groupKey: 0oa1b2c3d4e5f6g7h8                       │
         │          schedule: { frequency: { minutes: 30 } }           │
         │                                                             │
         │  # Auto-creates Backstage Group entities from Okta groups,  │
         │  # and User entities from Okta org members                  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.3 Custom Sign-In Resolvers
         - When built-in resolvers aren't enough
         - Building a custom sign-in resolver
         - Mapping external identities to Backstage
         [CUSTOM SIGN-IN RESOLVER - Real Code]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  // packages/backend/src/extensions/auth.ts                 │
         │  const customResolver = async (info, ctx) => {              │
         │    const { profile } = info.result;                         │
         │    const userEntityRef = stringifyEntityRef({               │
         │      kind: 'User',                                          │
         │      name: profile.email.split('@')[0],                     │
         │    });                                                      │
         │    return ctx.issueToken({                                  │
         │      claims: { sub: userEntityRef, ent: [userEntityRef] },  │
         │    });                                                      │
         │  };                                                         │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.4 Choosing Between LDAP, Okta/Azure AD, and Custom Resolvers
         - Same goal (map an external identity to a Backstage User), three routes
         - Decision guide for which one fits your org
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  IF YOUR ORG HAS...                             USE                        SEE        │
         │  ----------------------------------------------  --------------------------  ------   │
         │  An on-prem Active Directory / OpenLDAP            LDAP ingestion              24.1   │
         │  Okta or Azure AD as the identity provider           Okta/Azure AD group sync   24.2  │
         │  A homegrown identity system or unusual mapping       Custom sign-in resolver   24.3  │
         │  No IdP at all yet (local dev, small team)              Guest auth (Ch 22)      22.x  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.5 Identity Provider Troubleshooting
         - The most common failures when wiring an external IdP
         - Where to look first
         ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  SYMPTOM                           LIKELY CAUSE                     WHERE TO LOOK                         │
         │  ---------------------------------  -------------------------------  ---------------------------          │
         │  Users never appear in the Catalog    LDAP schedule has not run yet    Check catalog processing           │
         │                                                                        logs, force a manual refresh       │
         │  Sign-in works but no Groups           bind DN lacks read access to     Verify bind account can           │
         │                                          the groups OU                   read ou=groups (24.1)            │
         │  Login loops back to the sign-in page    Custom resolver threw and       Check backend logs for the       │
         │                                            issueToken never ran            resolver stack trace (24.3)    │
         │  Okta sync stops updating                  API token expired or           Rotate OKTA_API_TOKEN,          │
         │                                              revoked                        confirm scope is still valid  │
         └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    24.6 CBA Exam: Chapter 24 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: LDAP, Okta, custom resolvers

         │  # Install: yarn --cwd packages/app add @backstage/plugin-github-actions  │

         │                                                                           │

Chapter 26: Production Deployment & Operations
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26.1 Production Checklist
         - Database: PostgreSQL (not SQLite)
         - Auth: SSO (not guest access)
         - Secrets: External secret management
         - Monitoring: Logs + metrics + alerts
         - Backups: Database backup strategy
         - SSL/TLS: HTTPS everywhere
         - Scaling: Multiple replicas, resource limits
         [PRODUCTION CHECKLIST]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  ITEM              REQUIREMENT                                           │
         │  -----------------  ------------------------------------------------     │
         │  Database            PostgreSQL, NEVER SQLite                            │
         │  Auth                 Real SSO provider, NEVER guest access              │
         │  Secrets               K8s Secrets or vault, NEVER hardcoded in YAML     │
         │  Monitoring             Prometheus + Grafana + Alertmanager wired up     │
         │  Backups                  Automated PostgreSQL backup + tested restore   │
         │  TLS                        HTTPS everywhere, valid certificates         │
         │  Scaling                      3+ replicas, resource requests/limits set  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.2 Database — PostgreSQL Setup
         - Why PostgreSQL for production
         - Connection configuration
         - Migration management
         - Backup and restore
         [POSTGRESQL PRODUCTION CONFIGURATION]
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  backend:                                                              │
         │    database:                                                           │
         │      client: pg                                                        │
         │      connection:                                                       │
         │        host: ${POSTGRES_HOST}                                          │
         │        port: 5432                                                      │
         │        user: ${POSTGRES_USER}                                          │
         │        password: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD}                                  │
         │        ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: true }                               │
         │      pluginDivisionMode: database   # DEFAULT: own DB per plugin       │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.3 TechDocs in Production
         - Production publishers: Google Cloud Storage, AWS S3, Azure Blob
         - Builder: Docker-based for security isolation
         - Storage lifecycle: Cleanup and retention
         [PRODUCTION TECHDOCS CONFIGURATION]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  techdocs:                                                           │
         │    builder: external          # CI/CD builds, not Backstage itself   │
         │    generator: { runIn: docker }                                      │
         │    publisher:                                                        │
         │      type: awsS3                                                     │
         │      awsS3: { bucketName: easypj-techdocs-prod, region: us-east-1 }  │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.4 Search in Production
         - Elasticsearch or OpenSearch for production search
         - Search index configuration
         - Reindexing schedule
         [SEARCH CONFIGURATION - Production]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  search:                                                                 │
         │    collators: { catalog: { schedule: { frequency: { minutes: 10 } } } }  │
         │                                                                          │
         │  # For production scale, swap the default in-memory Lunr index for       │
         │  # @backstage/plugin-search-backend-module-elasticsearch or OpenSearch   │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.5 Backstage Upgrades
         - Upgrade strategy: Minor vs Major versions
         - The Backstage upgrade helper
         - Testing upgrades before production
         - Rollback strategy
         [UPGRADE CHECKLIST]
         ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  STEP                                            WHY                        │
         │  -----------------------------------------------  -------------------       │
         │  Run the backstage-cli versions:bump              Updates all @backstage    │
         │                                                   packages together         │
         │  Read the upgrade helper diff                       Shows breaking config   │
         │  (backstage.github.io/upgrade-helper)             changes to apply          │
         │  Test in a staging environment first                 Catches breakage       │
         │  Run yarn tsc + yarn test                              before it hits prod  │
         │  Deploy during a low-traffic window                     Minimizes impact    │
         │  Have a rollback plan (previous image tag ready)          Safety net        │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.6 Zero-Downtime Deployment Strategy
         - Rolling updates so users never see a gap
         - Readiness probes are what make this safe
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  # Deployment - rolling update with a readiness gate                     │
         │  spec:                                                                   │
         │    replicas: 3                                                           │
         │    strategy:                                                             │
         │      type: RollingUpdate                                                 │
         │      rollingUpdate: { maxUnavailable: 0, maxSurge: 1 }                   │
         │    template:                                                             │
         │      spec:                                                               │
         │        containers:                                                       │
         │          - name: backstage                                               │
         │            readinessProbe:                                               │
         │              httpGet: { path: /healthcheck, port: 7007 }                 │
         │              initialDelaySeconds: 10                                     │
         │              periodSeconds: 5                                            │
         │                                                                          │
         │  # maxUnavailable: 0 means the old pod is only killed AFTER the new one  │
         │  # passes its readiness probe - zero requests are ever dropped.          │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.7 Disaster Recovery Runbook
         - What to do when production Backstage goes down hard
         - Order of operations, not a wall of options
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  1. Confirm the blast radius: is it Backstage, or is PostgreSQL itself down?   │
         │  2. If DB is down: restore the latest automated PostgreSQL snapshot (26.2)     │
         │  3. If Backstage pods are crash-looping: roll back to the last known-good      │
         │     image tag via ArgoCD/GitOps revert (Ch 21) - do NOT hotfix in prod         │
         │  4. Re-run the upgrade helper diff (26.5) if the crash follows a version bump  │
         │  5. Once healthy: verify Catalog count matches pre-incident baseline           │
         │  6. Write the incident timeline while it is fresh - feed it into 27.x alerts   │
         └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    26.8 CBA Exam: Chapter 26 Practice Questions (10 Questions)
         - Q1-Q10: Production setup, database, upgrades


Chapter 27: Monitoring, Logging & Observability
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27.1 Monitoring Backstage Itself
         - Health check endpoint: /healthcheck
         - Note: the current default Root Health Service exposes readiness/liveness
           at /.backstage/health/v1/readiness and /.backstage/health/v1/liveness
           (JSON {"status":200,"payload":{"status":"ok"}}); /healthcheck is only
           present if you registered it yourself as a custom backend route
         - Prometheus metrics: the officially recommended path is OpenTelemetry-based
           instrumentation, which exposes metrics on a separate port at
           localhost:9464/metrics by default (not on the main API under /api/metrics);
           the older prom-client-based /metrics endpoint is being phased out
         - Key metrics: Request rate, error rate, latency
         - Catalog ingestion metrics
         [CODE BLOCK: Prometheus configuration]

         [OBSERVABILITY STACK — Full Monitoring Architecture]

         │  │ API response time   Prometheus     > 500ms p95 → P2     ││

         │  │ Catalog ingestion   Backstage      Failed > 5min → P2   ││
         │  │ DB connections      PostgreSQL     > 80% pool → P3      ││

         [REAL-LIFE: Setting Up Basic Monitoring for Backstage]
         ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
         │  $ curl https://backstage.easypj.com/healthcheck             │
         │  {"status":"ok"}  ← First thing to check when debugging      │

         │  # Prometheus metrics endpoint (built into Backstage):       │
         │  $ curl https://backstage.easypj.com/api/metrics             │
         │  # Returns metrics like:                                     │
         │  backstage_http_requests_total{status="200"} 15423           │
         │  backstage_http_request_duration_seconds_count 15423         │
         │  catalog_entity_count{kind="Component"} 47                   │
         │  catalog_ingestion_duration_seconds_sum 12.5                 │
         │                                                              │
         │  # Quick Health Check Script (add to your CI/CD):            │
         │  #!/bin/bash                                                 │
         │  STATUS=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" \          │
         │    https://backstage.easypj.com/healthcheck)                 │
         │  if [ "$STATUS" != "200" ]; then                             │
         │    echo "❌ Backstage is DOWN! Status: $STATUS"              │
         │    exit 1                                                    │
         │  fi                                                          │
         │  echo "✅ Backstage is healthy"                              │
         └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

    27.2 Logging in Backstage

         │      level: info          # error | warn | info | debug             │
         │      format: json          # structured logs, easy to ship          │
         │                                                                     │
         │  # Ship JSON logs to your aggregator (Loki, ELK) via your K8s       │
         │  # log driver / DaemonSet - Backstage itself just writes to stdout  │
         └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
